# Verified facts

Every entry names how it was established. A path is a claim someone can re-check in a minute; a
live measurement is a claim that decays. Trust neither kind blindly after a dependency bump.

## Measured against a running system

Carried over from the fork README's live checks (2026-08). Re-measure before relying on any of it.

| Fact                                      | Result                                                   |
| ----------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------- |
| Node in the container                     | v24.16 / v24.18 — the server needs ^24.13                |
| Agents installed                          | `claude` 2.1.241, `codex` 0.139.0                        |
| Headless `claude -p` in the container     | works                                                    |
| `zerops_*` MCP tools from headless claude | works — `zerops_discover` returned the project name      |
| Public HTTPS per container                | HTTP/2, ~86 ms, TLS terminated at the Zerops L7          |
| WebSocket upgrade                         | already in zcp's nginx template                          |
| Zerops API from a browser                 | `access-control-allow-origin: *` — no proxy needed       |
| Server footprint in the container         | ~110 MB (2 GB box; wants 4 GB with an agent running)     |
| White on brand teal `#00b1a3`             | 2.61:1 — below AA, hence the deepened `#007e72` (4.82:1) |

## Measured against the `eval` project (2026-08-27)

Project `eval` `Rd2bffa7R32zxR2q5UeCcA`, its `zcp` service `EXiSBjznSNeZe4S3klsXnA`, over an active
project VPN. Re-runnable with a project-scoped API token.

| Fact                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | How it was established                                                                                                                               |
| -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **The L7 completes a WebSocket upgrade on a non-8080 declared port.** A throwaway `wsprobe` service (Bun, `alpine/bun@1.3.9`) declared ports 8080 and 3773, both `httpSupport: true`, running the same `Bun.serve` echo handler on both. `curl --http1.1` with upgrade headers to `wss://wsprobe-2333-3773.prg1.zerops.app/` returned `HTTP/1.1 101 Switching Protocols`, identical to 8080. A real client (Node's `WebSocket`) completed the handshake in 24–53 ms and echoed a message round-trip in 6 ms on both ports, concurrently — nothing here is 8080-specific.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | created and deleted `wsprobe` (`Ch2jYZL1Qyqc35aHphOLSQ`) in `eval`; see `questions.md` history, Q-09 removed                                         |
| **Adding a declared port to a running service means redeploying, and redeploying replaces the container.** There is no `PUT`/`PATCH` on `/service-stack/{id}` — `ports`, `requestedPorts`, and `customPortsEnabled` are read-only there. The only way to add a port is to list it under `run.ports` in `zerops.yml` and redeploy (`POST` through `zcli service deploy`, i.e. the same build/deploy pipeline as any code change). Proven on `wsprobe`: redeploying with a third port (4001) changed `GET /service-stack/{id}/container` from container `BCbXggxJRJqVQRCKzxrniA` (`wsprobe-runtime-1`) to a brand-new `sS73clQOSWe92yNiU0B2BQ` (`wsprobe-runtime-2`), and a WebSocket held open across the redeploy died with close code 1006 mid-build, ~33 s in.                                                                             | redeployed `wsprobe` with an extra port while a live WS client polled it every second; diffed `/container` before and after                          |
| **`requestedPorts` is not how a port gets added on a `customPortsEnabled: true` service type, and adding a raw (non-HTTP) TCP port doesn't touch it either.** Declaring a fourth `wsprobe` port with no `httpSupport` (`protocol: tcp` only) landed it directly in `ports` with `scheme: tcp` on the next redeploy; `requestedPorts` stayed `{isActive: false, requestedPorts: []}` throughout every redeploy. `customPortsEnabled` instead tracks the _service type_: `true` on every user runtime we checked (`bun`, `alpine`, `zcp`), `false` on the `core` build-runtime type. `/service-stack/{id}/public-port-routing` (`POST RequestPublicPortRouting`) is a separate mechanism — raw TCP/UDP port-forwarding with a public/internal port pair and a firewall IP allowlist, not HTTP subdomain routing, and unrelated to `run.ports`. | same `wsprobe` redeploys; read `RequestPublicPortRouting`, `BaseRequestedPortsJsonObject`, and the `ResponseServiceStack` schema in the OpenAPI spec |
| **`VSCODE_PASSWORD` is set and readable straight from the API** — `GET /service-stack/{id}/user-data` returns it in `content`, unmasked. The `/user-data/{id}/reveal` endpoint is not needed.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | listed the service's user-data as a normal token holder                                                                                              |
| **The full auth chain works from an external client.** `GET /` on the public URL 302s to `/zcp-login`; `GET /zcp-auth/<password>` returns `Set-Cookie: __zcp_auth=…; SameSite=None; Secure; Partitioned` and 302s to `/`; that cookie then reaches code-server through the L7.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | curl, no browser involved                                                                                                                            |
| **Non-8080 declared ports are publicly routed.** The `app` service declares only port 3000 and answers on `https://app-2333-3000.prg1.zerops.app`. Both ports carry `scheme: http` and there is no per-port WebSocket setting.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | fetched both service objects and the URL                                                                                                             |
| **The service env carries the full container URL.** `zeropsSubdomain` = `https://zcp-2333-8080.prg1.zerops.app` — scheme, host, port and region, not a prefix.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | service user-data                                                                                                                                    |
| **The project object carries the region too.** `zeropsSubdomainHost` = `"2333"` (a bare prefix, as documented) but `publicZone` = `fte23prpara6p2koq60b9pvsgk0.prg1-zerops.zone` — the region is derivable from it. Its first label is also the VPN routing domain.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | `GET /project/{id}`                                                                                                                                  |
| **Services are supervised declaratively via `startCommands` in the service YAML**, not only by imperative `zsc unit create`. zcp's own nginx and code-server are both `startCommands` entries.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | `ZEROPS_YAML` in the service env                                                                                                                     |
| **A dynamically created `zsc` unit is a real enabled systemd unit on the rootfs** — `zerops@sshfs-app.service`, `UnitFileState=enabled`, `FragmentPath=/usr/lib/systemd/system/…`, created with `ZCP_SSHFS_HOSTNAMES` unset. Whether it returns after a restart is still open, see `questions.md` Q-01.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | SSH into the container                                                                                                                               |
| The `eval` zcp already runs a z3 server on `127.0.0.1:3773`, bound to loopback — hence the SSH tunnel in `poc/pair.sh`. Container uptime at time of writing was 13 days, so nothing there has been restart-tested.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | `ss -ltnp` over SSH                                                                                                                                  |

## Measured against project `p` (2026-08-27)

Project `p` `uHqwF26oTQ6V8D86HeABIA`, a real `zcp@1` service `z3probe` (`Nq49woUlTBeFYdP0jg4DQA`), left running —
see `questions.md` history and the team-lead thread for its credentials. Not `eval`; `EXiSBjznSNeZe4S3klsXnA` was
never written to.

| Fact                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | How it was established                                                                                                                                                                             |
| --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **`ports` is not a field on any service-creation/import schema — declaring it at the top level of an import YAML is silently dropped, not rejected.** `RequestPostServiceStack`, `RequestPostStandardServiceStack`, and `RequestServiceStackImport` (the `yaml` field's shape, matching the public import-YAML reference's "all possible configuration options" example) have no `ports` key anywhere; the only place a port list exists in any schema is nested at `zeropsYaml.run.ports`. Importing `z3probe` (`type: zcp@1`) with a top-level `ports: [8080, 3773]` succeeded with no error, but the resulting service sat at `READY_TO_DEPLOY` with `ports: []` until it was actually deployed.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | `zcli project service-import` with a top-level `ports:` key against project `p`; `GET /service-stack/{id}` before any deploy                                                                       |
| **`zcp@1` can declare a second port exactly like any other `USER`-category runtime — the type carries no platform-side cap.** Read `eval`'s real zcp recipe (`GET /service-stack/{id}/user-data`, `ZEROPS_YAML`, read-only) to get its exact shape — `build.base: ubuntu@22.04`, `run.base: zcp@1`, `run.ports: [{port: 8080, httpSupport: true}]`, the `curl .../install.sh \| sudo sh` + `zcp init` + `zcp init nginx` init sequence, `nginx`/`vscode` `startCommands` — then deployed the identical recipe onto `z3probe` with one added line, `{port: 3773, httpSupport: true}`. It went `ACTIVE` with `ports: [{port: 8080, scheme: http}, {port: 3773, scheme: http}]`, no rejection, no different behavior from a plain runtime.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | deployed the real `zeropsio/zcp` install recipe (`build.base: ubuntu@22.04`, `run.base: zcp@1`) onto `z3probe` with `run.ports` listing both 8080 and 3773; `GET /service-stack/{id}` after deploy |
| **The 3773 origin routes correctly at the platform edge; it 502s only because nothing in the container listens there yet.** `https://z3probe-2696-8080.prg1.zerops.app/` 302s to `/zcp-login` exactly like a real zcp instance (confirmed further by a working `/zcp-auth/<password>` → `Set-Cookie: __zcp_auth=…` round-trip) — this is a genuinely functional zcp install, not a stub. `https://z3probe-2696-3773.prg1.zerops.app/` answers `502` from nginx, because the recipe's `nginx.conf.tmpl` (see "Read from source" below) only binds 8080; nothing rebinds it for a second port. Getting 3773 to answer is a recipe/software change (start something on it), not a platform blocker — this closes the loop with Q-09's finding that the L7 itself upgrades and routes any declared port equally.                                                                                                                                                                                                    | `curl -sSI` and `curl -sSI .../zcp-auth/<password>` against both `z3probe` origins                                                                                                                 |
| **Each declared port is a separate origin, and therefore a separate cookie jar.** `z3probe`'s two ports resolve as distinct hostnames — `z3probe-2696-8080.prg1.zerops.app` and `z3probe-2696-3773.prg1.zerops.app` — following `{service}-{prefix}-{port}`. This is why z3 on its own port needs no `__zcp_auth` gate: the 8080 cookie cannot apply to it, and z3's pairing → bearer → WS-ticket chain is real auth. Previously asserted by the platform owner; now measured.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | `curl` against both `z3probe` origins after the two-port deploy                                                                                                                                    |
| **`GET /service-stack/{id}/user-data` responds `{list: [{key, content, type, sensitive, editable, internal, serviceStackName, …}]}`** — a flat array, not a keyed object. `VSCODE_PASSWORD` came back unmasked (`sensitive: false`); `zeropsSubdomain`'s `content` is newline-joined, one URL per declared port (`https://z3probe-2696-8080.…\nhttps://z3probe-2696-3773.…`). Confirms H-01/H-02's assumption about this endpoint's shape, used by `fetchZcpVscodePassword` in `apps/web/src/zerops/api.ts`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | `curl -H "Authorization: Bearer <token>" https://api.app-prg1.zerops.io/api/rest/public/service-stack/Nq49woUlTBeFYdP0jg4DQA/user-data`                                                            |
| **Before the H-02 pairing endpoint is deployed on a container, `GET /z3-pair/<password>` and `GET /healthz` both answer `302` to `/zcp-login` on the 8080 origin, not `404`.** `z3probe` (still on its original two-port recipe, no `z3-pair`/`healthz` locations rendered into `nginx.conf`) 302-redirected both paths — nginx's single-`location /` catch-all handles anything undeclared, exactly like a plain unauthenticated hit on `/`. `https://z3probe-2696-3773.prg1.zerops.app/healthz` (the z3 origin, not 8080) still `502`s, unchanged from the row above. `apps/web/src/components/zerops/connect.ts` treats an opaque redirect (`fetch(..., {redirect:"manual"})`) as its own "predates the endpoint" case for exactly this reason, rather than only checking for `404`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | `curl -sSo /dev/null -w "%{http_code}"` against `.../z3-pair/<password>` and both origins' `/healthz`, 2026-08-27, ~09:40 UTC                                                                      |
| **The whole browser-only chain works end to end — this is the POC's central claim, and it is now measured.** Against `z3probe` after the container-side deploy, from a laptop with no VPN and no SSH: `/healthz` on 8080 with no cookie → `200 {"initComplete":true,"z3Up":true}`; `/.well-known/t3/environment` on 3773 → `200`, `serverVersion` `0.0.35` (the pinned build); `/z3-pair/<wrong>` → `302` to the login page, minting nothing; `/z3-pair/<correct>` → `200` with `access-control-allow-origin: *` and `{credential, expiresAt, origin}`, a 12-char credential with a ~5 min TTL; RFC 8693 exchange of it at `POST /oauth/token` on 3773 → `200` with scopes `orchestration:read`, `orchestration:operate`, `terminal:operate`, `review:write`, `relay:read`; `POST /api/auth/websocket-ticket` → a 192-char ticket; `GET /ws?wsTicket=…` → **`101 Switching Protocols`** with a valid `Sec-WebSocket-Accept`. This answers the former Q-06 (Connect round-trip), which had never been exercised. | `curl` only, whole chain re-runnable with the zcp service's `VSCODE_PASSWORD`                                                                                                                      |
| **`POST /oauth/token` requires form encoding.** An `application/json` body is rejected with `415 Unsupported Media Type`; `application/x-www-form-urlencoded` succeeds. Anyone scripting the pairing exchange by hand will hit this first.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | `curl` against `z3probe`, both encodings                                                                                                                                                           |

### This repo

| Fact                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | Where                                                                                                                                    |
| -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **The browser brand-checks `fetch` against `Window`.** Storing the global as a class field and calling it as `this.#fetch(...)` throws `Failed to execute 'fetch' on 'Window': Illegal invocation`. React Native tolerates the unbound form, so code lifted from mobile passes its unit tests (which inject a fake fetch), typecheck and lint, then fails for every web user. Bind it: `globalThis.fetch.bind(globalThis)`. Found by driving the real browser, not by any test.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | `packages/client-runtime/src/zerops/api.ts`; regression test in `api.test.ts` emulates the brand check                                   |
| **A zcp container is identified by its service _type_, not its hostname.** `zcli` matches the literal name `zcp` because that is what its own flow creates, but `serviceStackTypeInfo.serviceStackTypeVersionName` is `zcp@1` on every container regardless of hostname, and it already rides on the stack response. Matching by name reports `zcp service is missing` for a project that demonstrably has one. Switching to type matching also revealed that two of this account's projects (`p`, `zleave`) hold **several** `zcp@1` services, which name matching had been silently hiding behind a wrong single-service answer.                                                                                                                                               | `apps/web/src/zerops/candidates.ts` `isZcpService`; observed live in the project list                                                    |
| **Mobile typechecks with `tsc`, not `tsgo`.** `apps/mobile`'s configured `typecheck` script is `tsc --noEmit` and is clean; running `tsgo` there instead reports ~58 spurious `TS2345 … type 'never'` errors on React Navigation `navigate(...)` calls across untouched files. Use each package's own configured checker.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | `apps/mobile/package.json`                                                                                                               |
| `t3 serve` has **no base-path flag** — mode, port, host, baseDir, cwd, devUrl, noBrowser, bootstrapFd, autoBootstrap, logWebSocketEvents only (the `--tailscale-serve` flags were removed at the freeze, 2026-08-29). z3 must own an origin root; it cannot be mounted under a path prefix. `cwd` is a trailing positional argument, not a flag; when `--base-dir` is unset it defaults to `~/.t3`; `--mode` is exactly `web` \| `desktop`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | `apps/server/src/cli/config.ts:173-198`, `apps/server/src/os-jank.ts:105-108`, `apps/server/src/config.ts:22-23`                         |
| **Correction, 2026-08-27:** the scriptable, `--json`-capable pairing mint is `t3 auth pairing create`, not `t3 pair` as this row previously said. `t3 pair` (`cli/pair.ts`) _discovers an already-running server_ via its persisted runtime-state file and prints a QR code — no `--json` flag exists on it at all. `t3 auth pairing create` (`cli/auth.ts`'s `authCommand → pairingCommand → pairingCreateCommand`) operates directly on the on-disk DB under `--base-dir`, needs no running server, and with `--json` emits `{id, credential, label?, scopes, expiresAt, pairUrl?}` (`pairUrl` only when `--base-url` is passed). `poc/pair.sh` and `hacks.md` H-02 already used the right command; `map.md`'s "z3 pairing chain" section still names `t3 pair` for this step. | `apps/server/src/cli/auth.ts:59-114`, `apps/server/src/cliAuthFormat.ts:27-67`                                                           |
| An environment is `{id, label, source, target: {httpBaseUrl, wsBaseUrl}}`; a paired remote server is a `BearerConnectionTarget`, no new type needed for a zcp container                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | `packages/client-runtime/src/environment/knownEnvironment.ts`, `connection/model.ts:18`                                                  |
| The endpoint contract already models `reachability: "private-network"` and `kind: "private-network"` — the VPN case is expressible upstream                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | `packages/contracts/src/remoteAccess.ts`                                                                                                 |
| The Zerops session client (real GUI login — email/password, TOTP, recovery codes, refresh with 401-coalescing — plus a non-expiring Integration Token as an alternative credential) is shared code now; H-04 is paid back. Mobile backs it with SecureStore, web with `localStorage`; a mid-2FA partial credential stays deliberately memory-only on both.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | `packages/client-runtime/src/zerops/`                                                                                                    |
| **2026-08-27:** `ZeropsSignIn` is now mounted in `ZeropsSettings.tsx` — signed out (or mid-2FA) it's the only content; signed in it renders nothing (by design) and `ZeropsProjectPicker` takes over. The old bare token gate is gone.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | `apps/web/src/components/settings/ZeropsSettings.tsx`                                                                                    |
| A post-sign-in sheet that lists environments and connects them already exists, built for T3 Connect's Clerk auth                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | `apps/mobile/src/features/cloud/ConnectOnboardingRouteScreen.tsx`                                                                        |
| Web first-run injection point                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | `apps/web/src/routes/_chat.index.tsx` — `HostedStaticOnboardingState`                                                                    |
| Only Electron main can spawn a local binary; `npx t3 serve` and hosted web have no such seam by design                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | `apps/desktop/src/ssh/DesktopSshEnvironment.ts`                                                                                          |
| **2026-08-27:** `filesystem.browse` (the atom `apps/web/src/state/filesystem.ts` wraps) already filters to directories only and needs no cwd for an absolute `partialPath` — calling it with `partialPath: "/var/www/"` lists exactly the directory entries under `/var/www`, `{name, fullPath}` each, no file/dir flag on the wire because everything returned already is a directory. No new RPC needed for the `/var/www` mounts panel.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | `apps/server/src/workspace/WorkspaceEntries.ts:191-238` (`dirent.isDirectory()` filter), `packages/contracts/src/filesystem.ts`          |
| **2026-08-27:** a project's `workspaceRoot` is on `OrchestrationProjectShell` and reachable per-environment via `useProjects().filter(p => p.environmentId === id)` — the connected environment's own workspace root is derivable client-side with no new request and no hardcoded path guess.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | `packages/contracts/src/orchestration.ts:445-458`, `apps/web/src/state/entities.ts` (`useProjects`)                                      |
| **2026-08-27:** `useZeropsCandidates()` only ever marks a candidate `"connected"` (and sets `environmentId`) by matching its derived z3 origin against an environment `useEnvironments()` already has (`environment.displayUrl`) — so a "connected" candidate cannot exist while `useEnvironments()` returns zero environments. In `_chat.index.tsx`, `HostedStaticOnboardingState` only renders when `environments.length === 0`, so the "exactly one connected project → auto-navigate" landing branch cannot fire from pre-existing state in that mount; it's exercised instead by the picker's `onConnected` callback resolving the same environment the instant a connect action completes.                                                                                 | `apps/web/src/zerops/candidates.ts:87-129` (`deriveZeropsCandidate`), `apps/web/src/components/zerops/landing/ZeropsSignedInLanding.tsx` |

### zcp

Read every `Where` in this section against `../zcp` on branch `feat/z3-container` — the z3 paths
do not exist on `main` (`README.md`, "The `zcp` half is parked on a branch"). Two rows also cite
`plans/research/zcp-install-delivery-architecture-2026-08-24.md`, which is an uncommitted working
file in `zcp` on `main`, on neither branch's history.

| Fact                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | Where                                                                                                                                                                   |
| -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| nginx listens on 8080 with exactly one `location /`, proxying to code-server on 127.0.0.1:8081; WebSocket upgrade and a 86400s read timeout are already in it                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | `internal/content/templates/nginx.conf.tmpl`                                                                                                                            |
| The public-URL gate is a cookie whose value is the raw `VSCODE_PASSWORD`; `GET /zcp-auth/<password>` sets it. **If the env var is unset there is no auth at all.**                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | `internal/init/init_nginx.go:54`, template lines 18-24 / 89-103                                                                                                         |
| `zcp init` runs as `run.initCommands`, so it re-runs on every container start and re-renders nginx.conf                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | `internal/init/init.go:56`, `plans/research/zcp-install-delivery-architecture-2026-08-24.md`                                                                            |
| Long-lived processes are supervised by systemd through `sudo -E zsc unit create <name> "<cmd>"` — the same primitive the sshfs mounts use                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | `internal/init/init_sshfs.go:52`, `internal/platform/mounter.go:83`                                                                                                     |
| Sibling services are sshfs-mounted at `/var/www/<hostname>` from that service's own `/var/www`, with no reserved-name list anywhere in the mount code — any sibling hostname can claim a path under `/var/www` (hacks.md H-21). Confirmed live: project `p` (home to `z3probe`) has a sibling literally named `app` — the reason z3's own workspace moved to `/home/zerops/app`, outside `/var/www` entirely.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | `internal/ops/mount.go:13`, `internal/init/init_sshfs.go`; `zcli service list --projectId uHqwF26oTQ6V8D86HeABIA`                                                       |
| Mount state is computed live from `/proc/mounts` + readdir + `systemctl`; **no manifest file exists**                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | `internal/ops/mount.go:260`                                                                                                                                             |
| `zcp@1`'s live `initCommands`/`startCommands` list is genuinely outside this repo — no file here is what a running container actually boots from, matching README's "platform-owned" claim. A checked-in MIRROR now exists at `deploy/zcp-container.yml` (adds z3/z3sidecar), reviewable but not itself deployed by anything — someone applies it on the platform side. It runs in two phases: every `initCommands` entry finishes (`curl install.sh \| sudo sh`, `zcp init`, `sudo -E zcp init nginx`, …) BEFORE any `startCommands` process (`zerops@nginx`, `@vscode`, …) launches — so nginx answering a request at all already proves that boot's `zcp init` reached completion (a required-step failure aborts the whole container start). This is why `/healthz` (hacks.md H-07) is only interesting for what happens AFTER that point, not as an "is the container up yet" signal.                           | boot journal table in `plans/research/zcp-install-delivery-architecture-2026-08-24.md` §1.1; `verified.md`'s own `z3probe` deploy row above; `deploy/zcp-container.yml` |
| `zcp service start <name>` (`internal/service/service.go`) is a package-level `map[string]execConfig` — the literal `ExecStart` every `startCommands`-declared long-lived process (`nginx`, `vscode`, and now `z3`/`z3sidecar`) runs, with signal forwarding and optional `systemctl set-property --runtime <unit> TasksMax=…` tuning shared across every entry. Distinct from — and layered on top of — the `sudo -E zsc unit create <name> "<cmd>"` primitive sshfs mounts use to get a unit registered in the first place; z3/z3sidecar do NOT use that primitive (hacks.md H-05, paid back) — `deploy/zcp-container.yml`'s `startCommands` registers their units the same way nginx/vscode already are. `z3sidecar`'s exec config self-execs: `binary: "zcp"`, `args: ["zcp", "z3sidecar"]` — `exec.LookPath("zcp")` resolves the same binary already running as this process's parent, same as any other entry. | `internal/service/service.go`                                                                                                                                           |
| **A `startCommands` entry is a structured object, not a bare string** — `{name: string, command: string (required), workingDir?: string, initCommands?: string[]}`. Pushing a plain string list (`startCommands: [nginx, vscode]`, as an earlier abbreviated read of `ZEROPS_YAML` suggested) fails schema validation: `yaml: unmarshal errors: line N: cannot unmarshal !!str "nginx" into zeropsYamlParser.startCommand`. The real value behind a bare name is `{name: nginx, command: "zcp service start nginx"}` — confirmed by getting this exact error from `zcli service push` against `z3probe`, then fixing it and deploying successfully, 2026-08-27.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | `internal/schema/testdata/zerops_yml_schema.json` → `properties.zerops.items.properties.run.properties.startCommands.items`                                             |
| **z3/z3sidecar supervised via `startCommands` survive a genuine service restart with zero manual steps** — `zcli service stop z3probe && zcli service start z3probe`, then polled `/healthz`: `z3Up` went `false` → `true` within ~10s of the container reappearing (`initAt` timestamp confirmed a fresh boot, not a cached response), no SSH, no re-running any init command by hand. A fresh mint (H-02) and the full pairing → bearer → ws-ticket → `wss://…/ws` 101 chain (see the row above) both completed again immediately after.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | `zcli service stop`/`start` against `z3probe`, then `curl` polling + the same chain as the row above, 2026-08-27                                                        |

### zcli

| Fact                                                                                                                                                                                                     | Where                                                                  |

| Multi mode means one WireGuard interface **per project**, named `zp<13 chars of base32(sha256(projectID))>` — not one interface with several peers                                                       | `src/vpnManager/tunnel.go:23`                                          |
| The tunnel is `wg-quick up <conf>`, shelled out with no sudo prefix, so the whole `zcli vpn up` process must be run as root. `wgctrl` is used only for key generation.                                   | `src/wg/wgquick_cmd.go:11`, `src/wg/darwin.go`                         |
| Root is needed for exactly three things: creating the utun, setting routes, and writing `/etc/resolver/*` (the latter is baked into the config's `PostUp`)                                               | `src/wg/darwin.go:200-218`                                             |
| `zcli vpn status --json` emits a stable schema with machine-readable reason codes, translated to prose only at the cmd layer                                                                             | `src/cmd/vpnStatus.go`                                                 |
| `vpnManager.Coordinator` is a renderer-agnostic Go library with two public methods, `Inspect()` and `Apply()` — `zcli-interactive` already drives it from a Bubble Tea TUI in-process with zero IPC      | `src/vpnManager/coordinator.go`; `../zcli-interactive/src/vpnControl/` |
| `zcli zcp [project] [agent]` already does the whole terminal-side flow: resolve project → find the zcp service → connect VPN → SSH → launch an agent, optionally in a persistent tmux                    | `src/cmd/zcp.go`, `src/zcpSession/`                                    |
| `Candidate{Project, Service, Agents, Reason}` is the existing model for "which projects are usable and why not" — reasons cover project inactive, no zcp service, several zcp services, service inactive | `src/zcpSession/target.go:24`, `catalog.go:171`                        |
| `docs/multi-vpn-architecture.md` on branch `kh-zcp-multi-vpn` is an approved implementation spec — read it before touching VPN                                                                           | `../zcli` on that branch                                               |

### Zerops API

| Fact                                                                                                                                                                                                                | Where                                                                           |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Session auth is `POST /auth/login` → `{auth: TokenData, user}`; TOTP and recovery codes both go to `POST /2fa/totp/login` in the same `token` field; refresh is `POST /auth/refresh` with `{refreshTokenId}`        | `../frontend-legacy/libs/zef/src/auth/auth.api.ts`                              |
| Integration Tokens (what `zcli login` consumes) are a separate, non-expiring, org-scoped credential — no refresh                                                                                                    | `../frontend-legacy/libs/models/src/integration-token/`                         |
| `/user/info` has **no top-level `clientId`**; membership is in `clientUserList` and a user can belong to several orgs, so a single org can look like an empty account                                               | `apps/mobile/src/features/zerops/zerops-api.ts`                                 |
| Projects are `POST /project/search` with a `clientId` eq filter; services are `GET /project/{id}/service-stack`                                                                                                     | same                                                                            |
| VPN is plain REST: `POST /project/{id}/vpn` with your own public key returns the project identity, your assigned IP, **and pre-rendered WireGuard config per platform**. Nothing in the handshake is zcli-specific. | Zerops Go SDK `sdk/PostProjectVpn.go`; call site `../zcli/src/cmd/vpnUp.go:270` |
| `connectedStacks` comes back empty on every service — there is no dependency topology to draw                                                                                                                       | fork README                                                                     |

| Fact                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | How it was established                                                                                                          |

| **Container baseline**: zcp `v9.152.0` (release), Node `v24.20.0`, npm `11.19.0`; Claude logged in (`~/.claude/.credentials.json` present), Codex not (`~/.codex/auth.json` absent); `~/.zcp/` exists (only `serve.log`), `~/.t3` does not.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | `ssh zcp` after `zcli vpn up nTV3oMB2SS634ImDJnQckg` — `zcp version`, `node -v`, `npm -v`, `ls`                                 |
| **A hand-pushed zcp binary takes over cleanly**: `make linux-amd` → scp → `sudo install` → sha256 match → `cd /var/www && zcp init` (all steps green, extensions reinstalled) → `sudo -E zcp init nginx` → `nginx -s reload` rc=0. `zcp version` afterwards reports the local commit (`v9.152.0-14-g2c677658`).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | `z3-dev-push.sh zcp`, then `zcp version` on the container                                                                       |
| **nginx runs as `zerops`** (master pid owned by zerops, `zcp service start nginx` → `nginx -g 'daemon off;'`, pid file `/tmp/nginx.pid`), so `nginx -t` / `nginx -s reload` work without sudo; `sudo nginx -t` fails on the pid file — do not sudo it.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | `ps -eo user,pid,args`, `nginx -t`, `nginx -s reload` as zerops                                                                 |
| **Supervision primitives**: `zsc unit` has only `create` and `remove`; units land as `zerops@<name>.service` (`zerops@nginx`, `zerops@vscode`, `zerops@zerops` present; sshfs mounts use the same `zsc unit create sshfs-<host> …`). Restart = `sudo systemctl restart zerops@<name>` (passwordless sudo works).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | `zsc unit --help`, `systemctl list-units --type=service --all`, `sudo -n systemctl status zerops@nginx`                         |
| **The fork packs and installs on the container's Node.** `vp run --filter @t3tools/web build` 11.7 s; `cli.ts build` (tsdown + client copy); new `cli.ts pack` (the publish rewrite → tarball, 0 `catalog:` refs left, 786 `dist/client/` files) → `t3-0.0.33-dev.<sha>.tgz` 18 MB. On the container `npm install <tgz>` into prefix `/home/zerops/.zcp/z3`: 198 packages in 55 s, 282 MB on disk, `node_modules/.bin/t3 -> ../t3/dist/bin.mjs`; `t3 --version` → `t3 v0.0.33` (the dev tag is visible only via `npm ls t3`), `t3 serve --help` renders. Native deps (node-pty) installed without a toolchain complaint. | `z3-dev-push.sh z3` log; `tar -xzOf … package/package.json`; `ls`, `du -sh`, `t3 --version`, `t3 serve --help` on the container |

| **The product branch `z3` (cut from `upstream/main` `4c51b4c9b`, T3 0.0.35 + 4 days) packs and runs on the container's Node.** `vp install --frozen-lockfile` (6 s; the POC-era `node_modules` lacked upstream's new `heic-to` and the web build failed on an unresolved import — a rebase always needs the reinstall, now part of the push script) → web build → `cli.ts build` → `cli.ts pack` → `t3-0.0.35-dev.<sha>.tgz` 20 MB → `npm install` into `/home/zerops/.zcp/z3` → `npm ls` `t3@0.0.35-dev.c952342cb`, `t3 --version` → `t3 v0.0.35`.                                                                      | `../zcp/eval/scripts/z3-dev-push.sh z3` from branch `z3`, 2026-08-28                                                            |

## S0 — measured 2026-08-28 (`../zcp/plans/z3-brief-2026-08-28.md` §6)

Every row names the brief's S0 number it answers. API base `https://api.app-prg1.zerops.io/api/rest/public`; `$TOKEN` = a user's access token, never printed. Laptop-only rows need no VPN.

### S0.1 — membership proof (Zerops API, two accounts: the owner = org KRLS `BkC8AGjFQMyFrLbzjHoE9g`, role OWNER; a non-member with its own org)

| Fact                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | How it was established                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **`GET /project/{id}` with the caller's token is the membership check, three-way on the status code alone**: member → `200` (body carries `clientId`, `status`, `publicZone`, `name`); valid non-member → `403` code `insufficientPermissions`; bad/expired token → `401` code `notAuthorized`. 30–40 ms each way (5× each). A made-up project id with a member token → `400` code `projectNotFound`, so "forbidden" and "does not exist" never blur.                                                                                                                                                           | `curl -s -w '%{http_code} %{time_total}' -H "Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN" $BASE/project/nTV3oMB2SS634ImDJnQckg` with each token; 2026-08-28                                                                                                             |
| `GET /project/{id}/service-stack` and `GET /service-stack/{id}` follow the same pattern (member `200`, non-member `403 insufficientPermissions`, ~30 ms; the non-member fails fast, no extra lookup cost).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | same curl shape against `/project/{id}/service-stack` and `/service-stack/gt7tJZjDSk2zyH5XvNeAQQ`                                                                                                                                                        |
| **`GET /user/info` has no top-level `clientId`** (confirms the code-read row above); `clientUserList[]` entries are `{id (= clientUserId), clientId, userId, status, roleCode, client:{id, accountName, avatar}, user:{…}}`. The only `roleCode` seen live is `OWNER`; the vendored zerops-go SDK's `ClientUserRoleCodeEnum` declares only `MANAGER` — the SDK enum is stale, never trust it for the role vocabulary. A user can sit in several orgs (the owner has two), so match the PROJECT's `clientId` against the caller's list, never "has any org".                                                     | `curl -H "Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN" $BASE/user/info` for both tokens (member: KRLS + a second org; non-member: exactly one org, zero overlap), ~37 ms                                                                                                |
| Role resolution is NOT free with the project GET: the role comes from `/user/info` → the `clientUserList` entry whose `clientId` equals the project's; a non-owner member can additionally be project-scoped via `GET/PUT /client-user/{clientUserId}/roles` → `{projectRoleList: […]}` (the mechanism zcp's `internal/platform/project_admin.go` `GrantSelfRole` uses). An org OWNER shows `projectRoleList: []` and still has blanket access, so an empty list means nothing on its own — `GET /project/{id}` `200` stays the ground truth. Reading another org's clientUser → `403 insufficientPermissions`. | `curl -H "Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN" $BASE/client-user/<ownClientUserId>/roles` for both accounts; cross-org id with the non-member token                                                                                                             |
| The search endpoints are membership-gated hard, not empty-result: `POST /project/search` and `POST /client-user/search` (body `{"search":[{"name":"clientId","operator":"eq","value":"<clientId>"}]}`, the `EsFilter` shape) with a clientId the caller does not belong to → `400` code `invalidUserInputWithText` "search is not allowed, insufficient privileges". So they cannot be used to probe, and there is no `/project/{id}/user` member-list endpoint anywhere (SDK + GUI grep).                                                                                                                      | `curl -X POST -H "Authorization: Bearer $NONMEMBER_TOKEN" -H 'Content-Type: application/json' $BASE/project/search -d '{"search":[{"name":"clientId","operator":"eq","value":"BkC8AGjFQMyFrLbzjHoE9g"}]}'`; member on own org: `200`, 19 projects, 57 ms |
| Token kinds behave identically for this check: the non-member's stored token and a fresh session token from `POST /auth/login` `{"email","password"}` (no TOTP demanded on that account; response `{auth:{accessToken, refreshToken, expiresIn: 432000, tokenType, userId, …}, user}`, 0.47 s) gave byte-identical `/user/info` and `403` on the project GET. Both are 66-char three-segment strings; the segment lengths differ per token (not decoded).                                                                                                                                                       | `curl -X POST $BASE/auth/login -d '{"email":"…","password":"…"}'` then the calls above with `auth.accessToken`                                                                                                                                           |
