## Turning the first CI run green — 2026-08-30

| Fact                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | Evidence                                                                                                                        |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **The SPI goldens were green on exactly one laptop, and the cause was dead code.** `redact()` rewrote any string equal to (or under) the RUNNING host's `cwd`/`homedir`/`tmpdir`, applied to fixture content recorded elsewhere. The Claude recordings carry the container's `"/tmp/cc-socks/164264.sock"`: on macOS (`tmpdir` `/var/folders/…`) it survived and matched; on any Linux host (`tmpdir` `/tmp`) it became `<TMPDIR>/…` and all four Claude goldens diverged at event index 5. Measured: across all EIGHT goldens there is not one `<CWD>`/`<HOME>`/`<TMPDIR>` placeholder — the path facility never masked a single character, it could only misfire. Removed whole (interface, implementation, its four tests, the one caller); every golden stayed byte-identical, and the suite passes on macOS and under `TMPDIR=/tmp` alike. A regression test now pins that redaction never consults the environment. Rule going forward: if a recording needs a path masked, mask it when RECORDING, never when comparing. | `TMPDIR=/tmp vp test run apps/server/src/spi/replay/` before (4 failed) and after (all green); `git status` on the fixtures = 0 |
| Nothing in the replay suite needs a live agent — that was checked, not assumed. Claude/Codex replay a static `.jsonl`; Cursor/Grok drive the real adapter against the scripted `scripts/acp-mock-agent.ts`; OpenCode uses an `OpenCodeRuntime` test double. That is why all three non-Claude baselines passed on a GitHub runner with no CLI installed. The step that DOES need a real agent — `spi/recording/record-claude.mjs` — is not in CI and must not be.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | CI job 99230261257 (10 tests, only the 4 Claude ones red) + the three replay modules                                            |
| **Three `cloud link proofs` tests asserted a branch no machine could reach.** `cloudLinkProofHandler` gates on `config.zerops` BEFORE it validates the endpoint origin (added by `0ba0597ad`, project-bound proofs), so an app built with no Zerops environment always answered `"This environment is not running on Zerops…"` and the origin rules the tests are named after went untested everywhere, laptop included. Reaching them needs the whole chain the handler demands: a Zerops environment, a resolvable public origin (`resolveZeropsLinkProofOrigin` gates next), and a bearer token — the Zerops door refuses cookie sessions by design (`browser_session_unsupported`), so the cookie helper these tests used cannot work there. All six cases green.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | `vp test run apps/server/src/server.test.ts` 147/147                                                                            |
| **`vp check` exits 1 on `main` — 11 `t3code(namespace-node-imports)` errors** in `ZeropsAgentAuthWatcher.{ts,test.ts}`, `ElectronProtocol.test.ts`, `record-claude.mjs`, `infra/relay/scripts/migrate.ts`. Pre-existing and identical with the day's work stashed; nobody had ever run the repo-wide check. Renamed to the `NodeX` namespace form (each rewrite asserting its own match count, since there is no AST tooling; the four prose references to Node's `fs.watch` API were left alone). `vp check` now exits 0 with 0 errors / 23 warnings.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | `vp check` exit code before (1) and after (0)                                                                                   |
| Day's tally on the first-ever CI run: **five independent pre-existing failures**, none introduced by the move, none visible on a dev laptop — unformatted fixtures, a stale SPI-version guard (2.0 vs the 2.1 the constant and `spi.md` both declare), a `release-smoke.ts` entry for the `packages/ssh` deleted at the freeze (still present untracked on the laptop), the tmpdir-coupled goldens, and the 11 lint errors.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | runs 33301432278 → 33305760166                                                                                                  |
| **The preload verifier still required what the freeze deleted.** `b36e895b3` made the desktop a pure hosted client, removing `getLocalEnvironmentBootstraps`, `pickFolder` and the Clerk passkeys bridge from `preload.ts` AND from the `DesktopBridge` contract — but never touched `verify-preload-bundle.mjs`, which demanded all three. Note `pickFolder` still exists in `ElectronDialog.ts` (the app-menu dialog service), so source presence alone would have read as a real bug; the contract settled it. The list now names four APIs the bridge genuinely exposes, so the script still proves the bundle executes and yields a working bridge — mutation-checked against an API that is absent.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | `git show b36e895b3 -- apps/desktop/src/preload.ts`; `vp run build:desktop` + the verifier, exit 0                              |
| Self-inflicted, recorded because the lesson is the point: narrowing that list broke `verify-preload-bundle.test.mjs`, whose synthetic preload still built the deleted bridge — the same follow-the-chain miss, one commit after fixing it. Cause was the verification, not the edit: `vp check` + typecheck + running the verifier itself, but not the package's tests. The rule that catches it is to run CI's own commands — `vp run --parallel --filter '!t3' --filter '!@t3tools/monorepo' test` and `vp run --filter t3 test`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | 4 red fixtures, then 9/9                                                                                                        |
| **`entrypoint.test.ts`'s symlink case has been red on every Mac and green on every Linux — the SPI-golden disease with the platforms swapped.** `makeTempDir` returned `os.tmpdir()` unresolved; macOS reaches its temp dir through `/var -> /private/var`, so the fixture's `moduleUrl` was `/var/…` while `isEntrypoint` correctly compared it to `realpathSync`'s `/private/var/…`. Linux has no such link (`/tmp`), so CI never saw it and only macOS developers did — meaning the whole `apps/server` suite had never been green on a laptop. Production code is right (a running module's URL IS the real path, as its comment says); the fixture now realpaths. Green with and without `TMPDIR=/tmp`, and pre-existing (reproduced with the day's work stashed).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | `vp run --filter t3 test` before (1 failed / 3405) and after                                                                    |
| **CI is green on `07c3d0d8c`**: 8 jobs green, `Mobile Native Static Analysis` skipped by its own `needs.mobile_native_changes` guard (no native changes), run conclusion `success`. Freeze checklist item 3's open question — GitHub-hosted runners — is answered: the whole suite runs on them, and the seven failures it surfaced were all pre-existing, none of them visible on a dev laptop.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | run 33306... jobs API                                                                                                           |

## The release manifest carried 342 MB of dependencies the bundle never loads — 2026-09-01

Measured on the published `zerops-code-0.1.7.tgz` (laptop, macOS arm64, isolated cold npm cache; the
container's own numbers are slower but the ratio is what transfers).

| Fact                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | Evidence                                                                                                                           |
| ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **The tarball declares 12 runtime dependencies; the emitted bundle statically imports exactly one of them.** Scanning all 10 chunks of `dist/` for statement-level bare imports yields `@ff-labs/fff-node` and nothing else. Everything the bundle still needs beyond that is reached through a runtime `require` — `node-pty`, `msgpackr-extract` (native), plus optional ones behind try/catch (`bufferutil`, `utf-8-validate`) and the bun entry points Node never takes. `effect`, the Claude SDK, `@opencode-ai/sdk`, `yaml`, `@pierre/diffs` and the Effect platform packages are all inlined. | `findStaticImportedPackages` (new, `scripts/lib/cli-external-packages.ts`) over the extracted 0.1.7 `dist/`                        |
| **Cold `npm install` of the released tarball: 159 packages, 500 MB, 9.2 s. With `dependencies` pruned to the three native roots: 7 packages, 158 MB, 2.7 s.** The installed binary runs in both (`z3 --version`), and the pruned one boots `z3 serve` and answers `/.well-known/t3/environment`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | `npm install --prefix … --cache <fresh>` for each, `du -sh`, `curl` against a local `z3 serve`                                     |
| **210 MB of the difference is a per-platform Claude binary the server never spawns** (`@anthropic-ai/claude-agent-sdk-darwin-arm64`, 192 MB, plus the SDK). z3 always passes an explicit `pathToClaudeCodeExecutable` pointing at the system `claude`, so the SDK's own binary is dead weight even when the SDK's JS is in use.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | `du -sh base/node_modules/@anthropic-ai/*`; `apps/server/src/provider/Drivers/ClaudeExecutable.ts`, `Layers/ClaudeAdapter.ts:4312` |
| **npm ignores `overrides` in an installed dependency's manifest — only the root project's count.** 0.1.7 ships `"@anthropic-ai/claude-agent-sdk>…-darwin-arm64": "-"` and npm installed that 192 MB package anyway. The block has been inert since it was added.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | the `@anthropic-ai` tree of the unpruned install                                                                                   |
| **A scan of the emitted artifact must require a word boundary after `import`.** effect's Schema codegen carries `importDeclarations: [\`import \* as Option from "effect/Option"\`]`as data; without`\b`the scanner reads that identifier as the keyword and reports`effect` as a runtime import, which would silently keep 47 MB in the manifest forever. Found by running the scanner over the real bundle, now a regression test.                                                                                                                                                                 | `findStaticImportedPackages` probe over `Schema-*.mjs` before and after                                                            |
| Note for anyone re-measuring `node-pty`: 58 MB of its 62 MB is `prebuilds/` for every platform, downloaded from npm either way. It is the floor for this delivery shape, and the reason a vendored tarball would move those bytes rather than remove them.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | `du -sh node_modules/node-pty/*`                                                                                                   |

## First real upstream intake — 2026-09-02

Ritual: `fork.md` §6. Last-reviewed SHA moved `f94a0d646` → `827345a07` (`intake.md`); SPI row 1
added (`compat.md`). 28 ports landed on `intake/2026-09-02` in five parallel worktree slices.

| Fact                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | Evidence                                                                 |
| ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| **The triage is mechanical and cheap.** `git merge-tree --write-tree --merge-base=<sha>^ main <sha>` simulates every cherry-pick in memory, touching no ref or worktree. Over the 180 post-freeze commits: 54 apply clean, 24 conflict only because an intermediate upstream commit is missing (chain-order, not divergence), 102 genuinely collide with the fork. Classifying a conflicted file as chain-vs-ours is one `git diff --quiet upstream-base-2026-08-28 main -- <file>`.                                                                                                                                                                                                           | `merge-tree` over all 180; per-file classification                       |
| **The fork's divergence is overwhelmingly in the client.** Of the 102 genuine collisions, 196 file-conflicts land in `apps/web` against 39 in `apps/server`; the hotspots are `ChatView.tsx` (18), `MessagesTimeline.tsx` (14), `MessagesTimeline.logic.ts` (11). 137 of 180 commits touch only client surfaces. Server-side intake is therefore cheap and UI intake is not — which is what made this intake server-only.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | path-overlap counts                                                      |
| **`git checkout upstream/main -- <dir>` is NOT a complete import** — it never deletes files upstream removed. `packages/effect-acp/test/examples/cursor-acp-client.example.ts` (deleted upstream by `08aad594f`) survived the checkout and had to be `git rm`'d. `imported.lock` catches it, because it pins the path's **tree OID**, not a file list. Import = checkout **+ delete what `git diff --name-status upstream/main HEAD -- <paths>` reports as ours-only**.                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | `imported-lock.ts --write` refusing; the `A` row in the name-status diff |
| Upstream also dropped three export subpaths from the imported packages' `package.json` (`effect-acp/rpc`, `effect-acp/terminal`, `effect-codex-app-server/protocol`). No owned code imported any of them, so the import was a no-op for consumers — but the export map is part of the imported tree and moves with it.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | grep across `apps/`, `packages/`, `scripts/`                             |
| **A pick can depend on an unported pick, and only the compiler says so.** `62d39bf00` (stop OpenCode child sessions) cherry-picks into an empty-context conflict because it calls `cancelIdleReconciliation`/`scheduleIdleReconciliation`/`interruptOpenCodeTurn`/`isOpenCodeAbortError`, none of which exist here. `git log -S <symbol> upstream-base..upstream/main -- <file>` names the sole introducer: `cb49e5d72`, post-freeze, not in the triage list. Ported it first, after which `62d39bf00` applied with **one** conflict (a deleted doc). The triage lists commits, not their dependency edges — a slice must be free to report a missing prerequisite rather than reconstruct it. | `git log -S`, `merge-base --is-ancestor`, the retry                      |
| **`vitest` does not typecheck, so a green test run hides a broken build.** `7e460f429` added `getEventReplayStats` to `ProjectionSnapshotQueryShape` and `716069f40` added `subscribeDomainEvents` to `OrchestrationEngineShape`; each upstream commit updated the doubles it knew about, but the fork carries one **extra** double in each of `CheckpointDiffQuery.test.ts` and `serverRuntimeStartup.test.ts`. Both files passed every test run and failed `tsgo --noEmit` with TS2741. **A port's verification must include the package typecheck, not only its tests.**                                                                                                                    | `vp run -F t3 typecheck` after a fully green battery                     |
| **`apps/server/src/server.test.ts` is flaky on `main` itself, not because of this intake.** 2 failures in 12 runs on the untouched pre-intake `main` (`b86307462`), against 1 in 21 on the integration branch; never twice with the same capturable test name, self-heals on rerun. It drives real websocket timing. Recorded so the next intake does not misattribute it.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | 12 runs on a detached `main` worktree vs 21 on the integration branch    |
| **`SPI_UPDATE_GOLDENS=1` writes unformatted JSON** (arrays one element per line); without a `vp fmt` pass afterwards the diff reads like a semantic change when it is whitespace. Corollary, proven twice this intake: **root-cause a golden divergence before regenerating.** `cb49e5d72` moved the OpenCode golden only because `replay/openCodeReplay.ts`'s fake SDK client lacked the `server.connected` event and the `permission.list`/`question.list` stubs the adapter's new startup path calls — completing the fake restored the golden with zero changes.                                                                                                                           | both regen attempts; `git diff` on the fixtures                          |
| **The Claude model catalog now lives in the manifest, not in code.** `035428368` turns `model-manifest.json` into the complete Claude catalog (models, aliases, status, badge, capability profiles, per-model CLI version bounds). Because `MODEL_MANIFEST_URL` is fork-controlled (`zeropsio/z3` raw `main`, preserved through the port), **a new Claude model is now a JSON commit to the fork's `main` — no z3 release and no zcp pin bump.** Catalog after the intake: `claude-fable-5-1` (current, badge `new`), `claude-opus-5`, `claude-sonnet-5` current; fable-5, opus-4-8/4-7/4-6/4-5, sonnet-4-6, haiku-4-5 legacy; default chat `claude-sonnet-5`.                                 | `ModelManifest.ts`, `model-manifest.json` on the integration branch      |
| **`035428368` deletes the five static functions the SPI seam wrapped** (`getClaudeModelCapabilities`, `resolveClaudeEffort`, `normalizeClaudeCliEffort`, `isClaudeUltracodeEffort`, `resolveClaudeApiModelId`), replacing them with catalog-parameterized equivalents in a new ported-zone `provider/ClaudeModelCatalog.ts`. `spi/claudeProvider.ts` had to be rewritten, not re-pointed, and `spi/claudeProviderTest.ts` added so `textGeneration/ClaudeTextGeneration.test.ts` stops importing ported-zone fixtures across the zone boundary. The seam absorbing a driver rewrite without `apps/server/src/zerops/**` moving is the SPI working as designed.                                 | zone test 33/33 through every commit                                     |
| **A conflict hunk carries unported history, and taking it wholesale imports behaviour the fork lacks.** Resolving `035428368` in `composerProviderState.test.tsx` initially pulled in four test cases and an `unavailable` JSX prop that `git show 035428368 -- <file>` proves are **not** in that commit — they rode in from intervening upstream commits. Dropped in the slice; three of the four came back at integration once `cb49e5d72` (which adds the behaviour) had landed, and all 54 web tests then passed. **Verify a conflict resolution against `git show <sha> -- <file>`, not against the merged buffer.**                                                                     | `git show` per file; 54/54 after the union resolution                    |
| **`c78ae50a5` rejected on merit, not skipped by default.** Its cookie half is moot here — the cookie is set without a `Domain` attribute, so it is host-only, and every container answers on its own `*.zerops.app` hostname. Its `environment-id` half is already benign: `readPersistedEnvironmentId` returns `null` for an empty or whitespace file, so a truncated write self-heals by regeneration; what remains is multi-process convergence over one state dir, and z3 runs one supervised process per container. The two halves are not separable — the commit extracts `ServerEnvironmentIdentity` out of `make` precisely so the cookie name can depend on the environment id.       | `auth/utils.ts`, `environment/ServerEnvironment.ts`, upstream diff       |
| Pre-existing fork drift found in passing, **not fixed here**: `docs/user/source-control.md` still documents "Create Pull Requests While You Work" including GitHub/GitLab/Bitbucket/Azure PR support, while `apps/server/src/pullRequest/**` was deleted at the freeze. The doc promises a feature the product does not have.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | the file vs `git ls-files apps/server/src/pullRequest`                   |

## The repo is `zeropsio/mate` — 2026-09-02

Renamed from `zeropsio/z3`. The rows above keep the old name because they record what was true
when they were written; this row supersedes them for every live pointer.

| Fact                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | Evidence                                                                                                 |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **`raw.githubusercontent.com` is rename-transparent, and that is the hazard, not the comfort.** The old path answers `200` with **zero redirects** — GitHub serves the canonical repo under the stale name. So `MODEL_MANIFEST_URL` would have kept working silently. But a renamed repo's old name becomes **claimable**: the day anything is created at `zeropsio/z3`, that URL stops resolving to us and starts serving whatever the new repo holds, and `ModelManifest.ts` treats a bad fetch as a non-event (a failed fetch never fails a provider check). It is the only URL a running container reads data from at runtime, so it was repointed in the same change as the rename rather than left on the redirect. | `curl` on both raw URLs before and after; `api.github.com/repos/zeropsio/z3` → `301`, `.../mate` → `200` |
| **Release downloads survive on a real redirect**, unlike raw: `github.com/<owner>/<repo>/releases/download/…` answers `301` to the new name, and zcp's downloader follows it (`client.Do` on an `*http.Client` with no custom `CheckRedirect`, so Go's default up-to-10 applies). Repointed anyway — the redirect is a courtesy, and the pinned SHA-256 in zcp is what actually protects that path.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | `curl -L` on a transferred repo (`facebook/jest` → `jestjs/jest`); `internal/z3/z3.go:298`               |
| 23 files carried the old name. 22 were rewritten mechanically; `scripts/build-desktop-artifact.test.ts` was **missed by the obvious grep** because it holds the identity split across two fields (`owner: "zeropsio"`, `repo: "z3"`) rather than as one `owner/repo` string. Only the test run caught it. A rename sweep must grep the halves, not just the pair.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | the failing assertion, then 818 tests green                                                              |
| **The rename does not touch, and must not be assumed to touch**: the release asset name (`zerops-code-<version>.tgz`, already unrelated to the repo name), the npm workspace package `t3`, the bin `z3`, `BasePath` `/z3` and its nginx location, the systemd unit `zerops@z3`, `~/.zcp/z3{,.env}`, and `ZCP_Z3_{ENABLED,ALLOWED_ORIGINS}`. `BasePath` is the expensive one: `/z3` is baked into the published artifact (`release.yml`'s `VITE_BASE_PATH`) and `zcp`'s `z3.BasePath` must equal it, so moving the prefix is a coordinated two-repo change with its own release — deliberately not part of this rename.                                                                                                    | `internal/z3/z3.go:81`; `.github/workflows/release.yml:75`; zcp `spec-z3.md` C-6                         |
| The release workflow needed no change: it names the repo as `${{ github.repository }}`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | `.github/workflows/release.yml:174`                                                                      |

## 0.1.8 released and proven live on `z3-eval` — 2026-09-02

First release carrying the upstream intake and the release-manifest prune. Tagged `v0.1.8`
(`zeropsio/mate`), pinned by zcp `v9.159.0`. Everything below was measured on the real container
before the tag, not after.

| Fact                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | Evidence                                               |
| ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------ |
| **The catalog reaches the running server, which is the whole point of the intake.** Clearing `<stateDir>/model-manifest.json` and restarting `zerops@z3` made the server re-fetch from `zeropsio/mate`, validate the Claude adapter metadata, and persist all 10 models with their per-model CLI bounds — `providers.claudeAgent` survives the cache round-trip, not just `currentModels`. The first read after the push showed only `currentModels`, which was a **stale cache written by the previous 0.1.3 build** (the manifest TTL is an hour) — not the new code dropping the catalog. Check the cache's `fetchedAtMs` before concluding anything from its shape. | the cache file before and after a forced re-fetch      |
| **The per-model CLI version gate works, and it hides Fable 5.1 here.** `claude-fable-5-1` declares `adapter.claudeCode.minVersion: 2.1.257`; the rig's Claude Code is `2.1.251`, so the model is correctly withheld. A container with a newer CLI gets it with no release.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | `claude --version` on the rig vs the persisted catalog |
| **The whole release path works under the new repo name.** Deleting the install prefix and running `zcp init` downloaded `zerops-code-0.1.8.tgz` from `zeropsio/mate`, verified it against the SHA-256 compiled into zcp, and installed it: `current -> versions/0.1.8`, `zerops@z3` active, `/z3/healthz` 200, zero restarts.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | `zcp init` on the container after wiping `~/.zcp/z3`   |
| **The dependency prune is real in production**: the installed release has **10 packages** in `node_modules`, against the 159 that 0.1.7 installed.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | `ls ~/.zcp/z3/current/node_modules \| wc -l`           |
| **`pack`'s undeclared-import guard passed on the intake**, which is the check that the ports did not quietly add a runtime dependency: "Bundle resolves against 3 declared dependencies (10 chunks scanned)".                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | the dev-push build log                                 |

## Measured against `z3-eval` (2026-09-03) — chat output and observation

Project `z3-eval` `nTV3oMB2SS634ImDJnQckg`, from inside its `zcp` container with the container's
project-scoped key; a real `zcli push` of `weatherdash` (`EmWgeZ4rTiK0Ajpm8iH83A`) was polled
every 2 s through `GET /project/{id}/process`.

| Fact                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | Result                                                                                              |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **A deploy is blind for its whole duration on the mate side.** A 76 s `zerops_deploy` projects three rows: `tool.started`, one `tool.updated` with the arguments 160 ms later, then `tool.completed`. zcp emits MCP progress notifications while it polls (`Build <id>: BUILDING`, percent = elapsed/timeout), but Claude Code forwards none: 0 progress events in 34 transcripts.                                                                                                                                                         | The platform is the only live source while a tool runs.                                             |
| **The direct process list carries what the pipeline view needs.** A `stack.build` process embeds `appVersion.id`, `appVersion.status` (`BUILDING` → `DEPLOYING` → `ACTIVE`), `appVersion.build.{serviceStackId,pipelineStart,startDate,endDate,pipelineFinish,pipelineFailed}`, `activationDate`, and `prepareCustomRuntime` (null for a static runtime). Measured sequence: pipelineStart 14:14:16.8 → startDate 14:14:27.1 → endDate 14:14:27.9 → DEPLOYING ~53 s → ACTIVE at pipelineFinish 14:15:20.6; process `FINISHED` at 14:15:23. | `build.serviceStackId` (`makmWzfn…`) is the ephemeral build container's stack, not the service's.   |
| **The build log is reachable by tag.** `GET /project/{id}/log` returns a signed URL whose own query carries `accessToken`; `…&serviceStackId=<build stack>&tags=zbuilder@<appVersionId>&limit=8&desc=1` returned the build's lines ("✅ BUILD ARTEFACTS READY TO DEPLOY"). `desc=1` is honoured.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | Append params with `URL.searchParams.set`; the URL is a secret.                                     |
| **The log stream is a WebSocket of batches.** `wss://<same host>/api/rest/log/stream?<same query>&serviceStackId=…&limit=100&from=<newest item id>` completes the upgrade; each text frame is `{"items":[…]}` with the HTTP item shape. With `from=<id>` the first frames replayed older items, so the initial burst overlaps the HTTP backfill — dedupe by id.                                                                                                                                                                            | Whether `/stream` honours an ISO `from` is unverified; the GUI passes the last item id.             |
| **The server drops superseded `tool.updated` rows from history** (`ActivityPayloadProjection.ts` `dropSupersededToolUpdatedActivities`), so a reloaded thread sees only `tool.started` + `tool.completed` per call.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | A Zerops call's anchor must be its `tool.started` row, or the anchor moves between live and reload. |

## Measured against `z3-eval` (2026-09-04) — platform websocket from a browser origin, mount table, agent-browser

Project `z3-eval` `nTV3oMB2SS634ImDJnQckg`, org `BkC8AGjFQMyFrLbzjHoE9g`. The websocket rows were
measured from the laptop with a **user** token and `Origin: https://mate.zerops.io` on every request,
following the flow in zcp `internal/dataconsole/watch/watch.go`; the container rows over `ssh zcp`.

| Fact                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | Result                                                                                                                                                                            |
| ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **The platform websocket accepts a browser origin other than `app.zerops.io`.** `OPTIONS` and `POST /api/rest/public/web-socket/login` answer `access-control-allow-origin: *`; the upgrade to `wss://api.app-prg1.zerops.io/api/rest/public/web-socket/<receiverId>/<webSocketToken>` with `Origin: https://mate.zerops.io` returns `101` and the server greets with `{"type":"SocketSuccess"}`. Nothing stands between the hosted client and the push channel.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | Login → upgrade → greeting in 78 ms.                                                                                                                                              |
| **A list subscription is the search POST plus three fields.** `POST /service-stack/search` and `POST /process/search` with `{"subscriptionName":"<Entity>__list-subscription","receiverId":<uuid>,"wsOutputType":"listStream"}` return `200` with the current `items`, and the socket starts receiving `{"type":"search","subscriptionName":…,"data":{"add":[ids],"delete":[ids]}}`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | `ServiceStack__list-subscription` and `Process__list-subscription` both accepted for a `clientId eq` + `projectId eq` filter.                                                     |
| **`listStream` is membership only.** `PUT /service-stack/{id}/restart` on `weatherdash` produced one `Process__list-subscription` push with `add=[<process id>]` 1.5 s after the call; the process reaching `FINISHED` 16 s later produced no push, and the service's status changes produced no `ServiceStack` push at all.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | A push is a signal to re-read; status transitions still need a read (or an item-level subscription form, see the next row when measured).                                         |
| **`updateStream` pushes status transitions.** The same search POST with `{"subscriptionName":"<Entity>__update-subscription","wsOutputType":"updateStream","disableOutput":true,"receiverId":…}` (the official app's `updateSubscribe`, `frontend-legacy/libs/zef/src/entities/entity-manager-entity.service.ts:150`) returns `200` with no items and the socket then receives `{"type":"search","subscriptionName":"Process__update-subscription","data":{"update":[<entity>]}}`. Through one `weatherdash` restart: `Process` `RUNNING` 0.9 s after the call and `FINISHED` 24 s later; `ServiceStack` `RESTARTING` at 1.7 s and `ACTIVE` at 25.7 s. Each `update` entry is the full entity (`id`, `status`, `name`, …).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | Membership from `listStream`, status from `updateStream`; the client needs no poll while the socket is up.                                                                        |
| **Heartbeat**: `{"type":"ping"}` every 15 s is answered with `{"type":"pong"}`; the socket stayed open for 93 s with no other traffic.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | Same cadence zcp's watcher uses.                                                                                                                                                  |
| **sshfs mounts are visible in the container's mount table** as `<host>:/var/www /var/www/<host> fuse.sshfs rw,nosuid,nodev,relatime,user_id=2023,group_id=2023 0 0`, one line per mounted dev service (`s3git1`, `s3git2`, `weatherdash`), each backed by a `zerops@sshfs-<host>` unit.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | The repository set can be read from `/proc/mounts` with no platform call.                                                                                                         |
| **A service restart does not break its mount.** Through the whole `weatherdash` restart the `/proc/mounts` line stayed, `timeout 2 stat /var/www/weatherdash` returned `directory` every time (6–84 ms), and the sshfs unit stayed `active`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | A stopped (not restarted) service is still unmeasured; keep the bounded probe.                                                                                                    |
| **agent-browser in the container is `0.35.1`, one daemon per Linux user, session `default`**: `~/.agent-browser/default.{sock,pid,stream,target,config,version}`. Two `zcp serve` processes share it. Streaming is always on; the daemon binds an OS-assigned localhost port and publishes it in `~/.agent-browser/default.stream` (observed `44831`), the same value `agent-browser stream status --json` reports as `port`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | mate discovers the port by reading `default.stream`; zcp sets nothing. `AGENT_BROWSER_STREAM_PORT` exists for a fixed bind but is not needed.                                     |
| **`default.target` names the page the last batch left**: `{"targetId":…,"url":"https://weatherdash-26a7.prg1.zerops.app/","pinned":false}`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | A cheap "what is the agent looking at" read without touching the daemon.                                                                                                          |
| **agent-browser 0.35.1 output shapes, one live pass over `weatherdash`**: `network requests --json` → `{"success":true,"data":{"requests":[{"requestId","method","url","status","resourceType","mimeType","timestamp","headers","responseHeaders"}],"lifecycle":…}}` (plain form `[id] GET <url> (Document) 200`); `screenshot --annotate <path> --json` writes the PNG to the path (36 KB at the default viewport) and answers `{"data":{"annotations":[{"box":{x,y,width,height},"name","number","ref","role"}]}}`; `errors --json` → `data.errors[]`, `console --json` → `data.messages[]`. The field a _failed_ request carries (`failure`?) is unmeasured: no request failed in the pass.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | zcp's `parseNetworkRequests` reads `data.requests` and filters `status ≥ 400` or a non-empty `failure`; the status filter is proven, the failure field is inferred from `--help`. |
| **Subdomain URL shape**: port 80 carries no port segment, any other port does. `https://weatherdash-26a7.prg1.zerops.app/` → 200, `https://weatherdash-26a7-80.prg1.zerops.app/` → 502; `https://zcp-26a7-8080.prg1.zerops.app/…` → 200, `https://zcp-26a7.prg1.zerops.app/` → 502.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | `map.md`'s `{service}-{prefix}-{port}` derivation holds only for non-default ports; the client's origin composer omits `-80`.                                                     |
| **`network requests --json` is ≈1 KB per request** (every entry carries `headers`, `responseHeaders`, `postData`): docs.zerops.io 41 requests / 40 KB, www.zerops.io 118 / 123 KB, nytimes.com 471 / 845 KB, amazon.com 614 / 1.33 MB — the last is over `zerops_browser`'s 1 MiB stdout cap. `--status 400-599` (a range, per `--help`: `200`, `2xx`, `400-499`) returns only the 4xx/5xx entries, ~2 KB on those pages. A request that failed or is still in flight has **no `status` key and no `failure` field** in 0.35.1; the two are indistinguishable in the JSON.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | The batch tail asks for `--status 400-599` at the source; entries without `status` are not reported.                                                                              |
| **The S7 `zerops_browser` driven through the real MCP stdio protocol on the rig** (a linux build of zcp `main` run as `/tmp/zcp-dev serve`, `initialize` → `tools/list` → `tools/call`): 22 tools listed, `zerops_browser` input has `screenshot`; a call with `[get title] [set viewport 390 844] [is visible body] [eval 1+1]` + `screenshot: true` returned text JSON plus one `image/png` content block (390×844) in 4.1 s; `eval` was stripped, `set viewport` accepted, a failing `find` classified `selector-not-found`; the tail is `errors`, `console`, `network requests --status 400-599`, `close`. Inside `batch`, `screenshot --annotate <path>` fails ("Element not found: --annotate") and `screenshot <path> --annotate` writes no file; the plain `screenshot <path>` writes the PNG. A "nonexistent" page on a static service answers HTTP 200 (SPA fallback), so its empty network tail is correct; in the same batch nytimes.com yielded its 404 `.vtt`. | The annotate flag is dropped from the batch (zcp fix on `main`).                                                                                                                  |
| **The rig's mate is `0.2.1`** (`~/.zcp/mate/current -> versions/0.2.1`), behind the `0.2.5` pin in zcp `main`: the container's zcp predates the pin bump.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | A `zcp init` with a current zcp is needed before any release-path test here.                                                                                                      |

A default-viewport screenshot is therefore ~300 KB base64 on the wire; the SPI image cap must be above that. Codex is unmeasured.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | `/tmp/claude-image-probe.jsonl` on the rig, 2026-09-04 20:35 UTC                     |
