## Upstream intake `827345a07` → `c8f77e0d4` — 2026-09-05

Third intake (`intake.md` row 3): 47 ports + 8 recovered, landed on `main` as `e6273d2bf..635214806`
after the owned-core slice `b67a248bd..0aadb483c`. Facts measured on the way, none live on a rig yet.

| Fact                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | How                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **A byte-identical import of upstream's wire packages fails `vp check` under vite-plus 0.2.2.** Upstream moved to 0.3.0 (`f5fbb1bcb`), whose oxfmt breaks long union types one member per line; `vp fmt --check` on upstream's `schema.gen.ts` reports a formatting issue under 0.2.2 and passes under 0.3.0. The bump and the import therefore share one commit (`94f95dc21`, later `123ba0daa`).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | `git show upstream/main:packages/effect-codex-app-server/src/_generated/schema.gen.ts > …; vp fmt --check …` before and after the catalog bump                                                                                                                  |
| **Intake row 2 skipped eight ported-zone or client-runtime commits that were prerequisites of row 3's ports.** `snapshotForCwd` on `ProviderInstance` (`80a14b658`), `Drivers/instanceIdentity.ts` (`08aad594f`, imported by `AntigravityDriver`), `DPOP_ACCESS_TOKEN_REFRESH_SKEW_MS`/`expiresAtEpochMs` (`9e646ad84`), `serverConfigProjection` (`b883fc066`) and four more each surfaced as a `TS2353`/`TS2304` under a later port.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | `git log --format=%h upstream-base-2026-08-28..827345a07 -- apps/server/src/provider` against `git log --format=%s main \| grep '^port:'`; `git log -S<symbol> upstream-base-2026-08-28..upstream/main` per missing symbol                                      |
| **Before `c163d502d`'s port, a repository target whose turn diff exceeded `CHECKPOINT_DIFF_MAX_OUTPUT_BYTES` (10,000,000 B) was truncated silently and the checkpoint summary under-reported it.** `GitVcsDriver.diffCheckpoints` in patch mode uses `outputMode: "truncate"`; the unified-diff parser then reported a 25,000-line file (`"payload".repeat(64)` per line, ~11.2 MB) as `additions: 22222`, smaller files in the same patch exact. Numstat mode is per-file and uses `outputMode: "error"`, so the fork's multi-repo capture (`zerops/ZeropsCheckpointTargets.ts`) now asks for numstat and an overflow fails loudly into `diffUnavailable`. An earlier claim that the summary came back as `files: []` was not reproduced and is withdrawn. | `apps/server/src/zerops/ZeropsCheckpointTargets.test.ts` "summarises every file even when one repository's turn diff is oversized" — real git repo through the real `VcsProcess`/`GitVcsDriver`/`CheckpointStore` layers, RED on the pre-port tree, GREEN after |
| **On Claude Code 2.1.251 the streamed `rate_limit_event` never becomes an `account.rate-limits.updated` after `19d8ab2ae`.** The CLI emits utilization only under `rate_limit_info.unifiedWindows.{five_hour,seven_day}`; upstream's normaliser (`provider/Layers/claudeUsageLimits.ts`, byte-identical to upstream) requires a flat `utilization` number and returns nothing otherwise. All four recorded Claude fixtures lose exactly that one event; the Limits tab is fed by the `get_usage` probe.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | the four `spi/fixtures/claude/*.expected.json` diffs at the port commit + the normaliser's guard                                                                                                                                                                |
| **Two Antigravity tests fail only on macOS.** `AntigravityInstallation.test.ts` "honors explicit paths" and `AntigravityAdapter.test.ts` "serves client file reads and writes only inside the session roots" compare an unresolved temp path (`/var/folders/…`) against its realpath (`/private/var/folders/…`). Byte-identical to upstream; CI is Linux.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | `vp test run` on this laptop vs. the CI run                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| **`vp check` fails repo-wide on a ledger table row committed without the pre-commit hook.** Twice on 2026-09-04/05 a `verified.md` row from another session widened a column and left the table unaligned; each needed a whitespace-only reflow commit before `main` was green.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | `vp fmt docs/internals/zerops/verified.md` → 5-line diff, `vp check` exit 1 → 0                                                                                                                                                                                 |

## Project tags as the grouping mechanism — 2026-09-05

Measured against the live platform API (`api.app-prg1.zerops.io`) with a throwaway account's own
token, org `y6tz5g4lQVaENpmlknyrRw`. Two LIGHT projects created for the probe and deleted after.
Settles the five unknowns that blocked the group model (a Zerops project = one environment; the
user's "project" = a tag group above it).

| Fact                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | Evidence                                                                           |
| ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **`tagList` is accepted on create and returned verbatim.** `POST /client/{id}/project` with `tagList: ["mate:g:…","mate:role:dev"]` echoes it back on the created project.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | create + read-back                                                                 |
| **`tagList` is returned on all three read paths** — `GET /project/{id}`, `GET /client/{id}/project`, `POST /project/search`. No path needs a second request to learn a project's tags.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | one read per path                                                                  |
| **`PUT /project/{id}` updates `tagList` in place**, body `{name, description, tagList}` → 200 with the new list. So adopting an existing project and pairing a production environment retroactively both work. **It replaces the list wholesale**, so every write is a read-modify-write or it deletes the user's own tags.                                                                                                                                            | PUT then re-read                                                                   |
| **`tagList` is searchable server-side** in `POST /project/search`: operators `eq` and `in` match against array elements (a hit returns the project, a miss returns 0 items). `like` is rejected — `invalid or undefined string operator like` — so there is **no prefix search**: a group is found by its exact id, and listing all groups means listing projects. `clientId` is mandatory in every search.                                                            | 5 queries                                                                          |
| **That search index lags.** A project created seconds earlier is absent from `POST /project/search` while already present in `GET /client/{id}/project`. Grouping must therefore be derived from the lag-free client list — a just-created environment would otherwise vanish from its own group exactly while the user watches it appear.                                                                                                                             | create → both paths                                                                |
| **No practical tag limit.** A 1024-character tag and 200 tags on one project were both accepted and stored. Karlos's length/count worry (2026-09-05 chat) is unfounded at those bounds.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | 7 PUTs                                                                             |
| **`POST /project/{id}/service-stack/import` is the generic import** — body `{"yaml": …}`, returns `{projectId, projectName, serviceStacks[{id,name,processes[]}]}`. `POST /project/import` and `POST /service-stack/import` answer `methodNotAllowed`; `POST /project/{id}/import` is `notFound`.                                                                                                                                                                      | probe + a real import of the `go-hello-world` stage tier (2 services, 4 processes) |
| **The import rejects a `project:` block** (`projectImportProjectIncluded`) and requires `services:` (`projectImportMissingService`). A `zeropsio/recipes` tier must be stripped to its services before it can be imported into an existing project.                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | both errors observed                                                               |
| **`zeropsio/recipes/go-hello-world` is already organized by environment tier** — `0 — AI Agent`, `1 — Remote (CDE)`, `2 — Local`, `3 — Stage`, `4 — Small Production`, `5 — Highly-available Production`, each an `import.yaml`. The recipe repository already thinks in the same axis as the role tag, and its services carry `buildFromGit` into `zerops-recipe-apps/*` — the recipe is the source of truth for the superordinate unit, each app repo stays its own. | GitHub contents API                                                                |

## Gitea as an account-level tool — 2026-09-05

Measured by importing `zeropsio/recipe-gitea` twice on the throwaway account (org
`y6tz5g4lQVaENpmlknyrRw`), once as published and once with the region fix, then probing the running
instance. Both projects deleted after. Backs the "create Gitea" button and the menu section that
holds it (`tools.ts`).

| Fact                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | Evidence                                                                                                                     |
| ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **Import to a serving Gitea takes ~2 minutes.** `09:58:41` project `CREATING` → `10:00:43` `web=ACTIVE` with subdomain access, a 2 m 02 s wall clock: project `ACTIVE` at +20 s, `db`/`volume` `ACTIVE` at +41 s, then the build. The button needs a progress surface, not an indeterminate spinner.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | 20 s polling over project + service-stack reads                                                                              |
| **Transient build services appear in the project's service list** under generated names (`buildwebv1788602355`, `preparewebv11788602377`) and are flagged **`isSystem: true`**, as is `core`. Any reader of a project's services must filter on that flag or it will report a recipe service that does not exist.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | two polls during the build                                                                                                   |
| **`zeropsSubdomainHost` is `null` on the create response** and assigned within ~20 s. A client that reads it straight off the create call gets nothing.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | create + poll                                                                                                                |
| **The published recipe's `GITEA_DOMAIN` names a host that does not resolve.** `web-${zeropsSubdomainHost}-3000.app-prg1.zerops.app` → `NXDOMAIN`; the instance actually serves on `web-926-3000.prg1.zerops.app` (200). `app-prg1` is the API/zone spelling (`api.app-prg1.zerops.io`), not the subdomain one. `app.ini` derives `ROOT_URL` and `SSH_DOMAIN` from it, so Gitea reports its own root URL as the unresolvable host — every clone URL and web link it emits is wrong. Fixed upstream (PR on `zeropsio/recipe-gitea`); `giteaRecipe.ts` templates the region out regardless. | `curl` both hosts; `GET /api/v1/admin/users` 401 body carries `"url":"https://web-926-3000.app-prg1.zerops.app/api/swagger"` |
| **`${zeropsSubdomainHost}` in an import's `vault` is stored unresolved and expanded at runtime**, not by the import preprocessor: the service env holds the literal `web-${zeropsSubdomainHost}-3000.…`. So a recipe may reference `${zeropsSubdomain}` (the platform's own full URL, region included) and have it resolve in the container.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | `GET /service-stack/{id}/env`                                                                                                |
| **Gitea answers two useful questions unauthenticated** on its public URL: `GET /api/v1/version` → `{"version":"1.27.2"}`, and `GET /api/v1/users/search?q=` → `{"ok":true,"data":[]}` on a fresh instance. The first turns "the platform says `ACTIVE`" into "Gitea is answering"; the second makes "create the admin user" a step that can be ticked rather than nagged. `GET /api/v1/admin/users` is 401, as expected.                                                                                                                                                                 | 5 probes                                                                                                                     |
| **Creating the admin user and minting a runner registration token both need a shell in the `web` service** (`gitea admin user create`, `gitea actions generate-runner-token`). Registration is disabled and Gitea has no bootstrap endpoint, so neither is reachable from mate: the container is in another project and mate reaches no container but its own. Both stay user-owned steps with the command shown.                                                                                                                                                                        | recipe `README.md` §2/§5                                                                                                     |
| **`PUT /user-data/{id}` REPLACES a service's whole env set rather than patching one entry.** Updating `GITEA_DOMAIN` through it emptied `GET /service-stack/{id}/env` from 24 entries to 0 (`GITEA_DOMAIN`, `DB_USER`, `DB_PASSWORD`, `GITEA_WORK_DIR` and every `SYSTEM` var gone); the running container survived on its already-rendered `app.ini` and would have failed on next restart. Do not use it to edit one variable.                                                                                                                                                         | `stack.updateUserData FINISHED`, env re-read                                                                                 |

## Agent provenance — who signed the agent in — 2026-09-05

Not a measurement of an external system but of this tree: the seams that made the "whose
subscription does this turn spend" rule implementable without breaking an existing invariant.

| Fact                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | Evidence                                                             |
| ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **The door already retains the Zerops user id.** `ZeropsIdentityGate` mints a grant whose `subject` is the Zerops user id, and `EnvironmentAuth.AuthenticatedSession` carries it on every connection. `makeWsRpcLayer(currentSession, …)` has it in scope where `registerZeropsRpc` is called, so a handler can take it as a dependency without touching the RPC wire at all.                               | code read + the wiring now in place                                  |
| **The subject must never come from RPC input.** `ZeropsAgentLoginStartInput` is client-supplied; a client that could name its own subject could claim to be any project member. It is passed from `currentSession.subject` in `ws.ts` instead, and `registerZeropsRpc`'s doc says so.                                                                                                                       | `registerZeropsRpc.ts`                                               |
| **Provenance is recorded, never derived.** A credential file says nothing about who wrote it, and `ZeropsAgentAuth` deliberately never opens one (presence only). The single moment the fact exists is a login succeeding on an authenticated session, so it is written then, to `<stateDir>/zerops-agent-authorizers.json`.                                                                                | `ZeropsAgentAuthorizers.ts`                                          |
| **Absence is normal and must not read as an accusation.** A credential can predate the file, be copied in (H-24 does exactly that), or ship in the image. `resolveAgentOwnership` therefore has a distinct `"unrecorded"` state and only ever reports `"someone-else"` with both a record AND an identified viewer.                                                                                         | `agentOwnership.test.ts` — "never reports someone-else without both" |
| **It is a disclosure, not a gate.** Nothing blocks a turn: the terms allow one person's subscription across their own containers, so blocking would break the legitimate case. The client shows a line; `"mine"` shows nothing at all. **Corrected 2026-09-15:** no component in `apps/web` or `apps/mobile` consumes `agentOwnershipNotice` or `resolveAgentOwnership` — the notice is written, not shown. | `agentOwnershipNotice`; 2026-09-15 grep of both apps                 |

| **The container is ready ~2 min after creation; the whole tier ~2.5 min.** `zcp` `ACTIVE` at 15:16:56Z (+2:02), `db` at 15:16:11Z, `app` at 15:17:26Z (+2:32) after one build (`buildapp…`, STOPPED once done). The waiter started at `awaiting-container` from the known project id (`startProvisioningForProject`), reached health, exchanged identity and landed on a draft.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | service `lastUpdate`; browser landed on `/draft/…`                                                                                  |
| **A just-created environment is not in the menu until it has its container.** The reload fired right after the writes listed the project as `NEW` — no service yet — and `selectMateEnvironments` (membership = a `zcp` is present) left it out, correctly; the reload at identity exchange, when the project is `ACTIVE` with its `zcp`, put it in. `GET /client/{id}/project` itself was lag-free throughout: a rename via `PUT /project/{id}` was visible on the very next read of the list, the detail and even `POST /project/search`. Refresh reloads every mounted list (the sidebar's and the screen's), verified by resource timing: two list reads plus one service-stack read per project, ~70–160 ms each. | sidebar snapshot before (5 rows) and after (6, `Otto · Stage`); rename read-back on three endpoints; `performance` resource entries |
| **The fresh container's agent starts unauthenticated.** The landed thread shows `CODING AGENT SIGN-IN REQUIRED`: a new zcp carries no credential, so the first thing Otto needs is the agent-auth flow (§8) — creation does not, and must not, copy one in (H-24 is the eval hack, not the product).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | browser screenshot after landing                                                                                                    |

## `GET /project/{id}/export` as an interim recipe source — 2026-09-05

Probed with the throwaway account's own token against the projects created earlier today.

| Fact                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | Evidence                                                        |
| ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **The endpoint exists and answers `{ yaml }`**: a whole-project import document — `project:` block (name `copy of <name>`, `tags`, `vault`, isolation flags), then `services:`. `GET …/service-stack/export` is `methodNotAllowed`; `…/import-yaml` and `…/service-stack-import-yaml` are `notFound`.                                                                                                       | four probes                                                     |
| **It is not a scrubbed document.** Service vault values come back as `REDACTED` with `sensitive: true`, but the project-level `vault.ZCP_API_KEY` came back in clear. Anything derived from an export must strip every `vault:` / `envSecrets:` block before it is shown, stored or imported; `recipeFromProjectExport` does, and reports only the count.                                                   | export of `Mx0EAKnDTm2tTnA40uwpFw` and `nslPTX1QSDA3Ip58N8fZqg` |
| **It is lossy.** The stage tier imported into `Acme Docs - stage` carried `zeropsSetup: prod`, `priority: 10` and `profile: oltp-staging`; the export returns `db` as `type` only and `app` as `type` + `buildFromGit` (with `@main` appended) + `enableSubdomainAccess`. A recipe cloned from an export is the sibling's shape, not the published tier, which is why it stays a fallback behind the store. | import YAML (`recipeStoreSeed.ts`) vs export read-back          |
| **The agent container rides along** as `hostname: zcp`, `type: zcp@1`, with its vault, `verticalAutoscaling.minRam: 2`, `maxContainers: 1`. A creation imports its own container, so the clone drops every `zcp@` service; a project that was only ever a container clones to nothing.                                                                                                                      | both exports; `recipeExport.test.ts`                            |

in the client at the start.

| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **All six rows read `Idle` within 12 s of a page load**, with no click: the health probe (concurrency 4) answered `ready`, the identity exchange registered each container (concurrency 2), the socket came up and the thread shells arrived. The client already opens a socket per registered environment; registration was the only gap.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | roster snapshot 12 s after load; `useZeropsAutoConnect`                                   |
| **A deleted container's registration was reaped.** With its project gone from the account, the environment kept reconnecting and its cached project claimed the landing; after a clean candidates read it was removed, its project ref forgotten, and the "Failed to connect. Reconnecting" banner and its leftovers disappeared.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | landing before (`www` of the dead environment) and after (Otto's)                         |
| **The landing honours a named environment.** `/?environmentId=…` opened that environment's primary thread; a bare `/` waited on live sockets and picked among them only.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | URL read-back after connect; Otto's row current                                           |

The export carries no `zeropsSetup` and the service detail stores none (`publicGitSource.explicitSetup: null`), so the build ran under the setup named after the hostname, which the repository lacks. The wait now settles on `READY_TO_DEPLOY` and names the service; the clone option says which services will need a deploy. | project + service-stack + process reads on `5tdxFTNGR7K1VXI74OBqlg`; `stack.build FAILED` |

## The service card — live allocation and public routes — 2026-09-06

The right panel's service map rebuilt as the Zerops dashboard's service card compressed for a side panel. Measured with the throwaway account against `acme-docs-dev` (`Mx0EAKnDTm2tTnA40uwpFw`) and the GUI's own source (`zerops-migrate-legacy`, `libs/zui/src/service-stack-card`, `metrics-buttons`, `apps/zerops/src/modules/core/resource-statistics-base`).

| Fact                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | How it was established                                                                                                    |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **The dashboard's "1 container · Cores · RAM · Disk" strip is `POST /current-stats/group-by-search`, not anything on the service-stack read.** Body `{"search":[{"name":"clientId","operator":"eq","value":<clientId>},{"name":"projectId","operator":"eq","value":<projectId>}],"groupBy":"containerId"}`; answers `{limit, offset, totalHits, items[]}` with one item per container: `{clientId, projectId, serviceStackId, serviceId, containerId, cpu:{limit,used}, vCpu:{limit,used}, ramGBytes:{limit,used}, diskGBytes:{limit,used}}`. `vCpu` is the shared-CPU pair, `cpu` the dedicated one (`0/0` on a shared service). ~110 ms. The GUI's `groupBy` enum is `serviceStackId`/`serviceId`/`containerId`; grouped by stack the same numbers come back summed with no container count, which is why the client groups by container and counts. | `curl` with the account's bearer token; the GUI's `ResourceStatisticsBaseApi.groupSearchCurrent$` and `StatisticsGroupBy` |
| **The `clientId` filter is mandatory**: the same body without it answers `400 invalidUserInputWithText "clientId: not defined"`. The GUI also sends `{"name":"billingEnabled","operator":"is","value":true}`, which drops the project's system `core` stack (1 item instead of 2 on `acme-docs-dev`); the client sends no such filter and drops `isSystem` stacks itself, as the topology already does.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | the two calls, one after the other                                                                                        |
| **The figures the dashboard prints are the `limit`s**: `acme-docs-dev`'s zcp container reads `vCpu 2 (used 0.076)`, `ramGBytes 2.625 (used 0.512)`, `diskGBytes 2 (used 0.161)`; the GUI's tile prints the limit with `number:'1.0-3'` and fills a bar with `used / limit`. `beviro-crm-dev`'s zcp read `2.75 GB` of RAM the same way, the figure the owner's own dashboard shows for a zcp.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | the response above; `metrics-buttons.component.html`                                                                      |
| **What a plain service list already carries per service** (no extra read): `versionNumber` (`v22.22.3`), `serviceStackTypeInfo.serviceStackTypeName` (`Node.js`), `mode` (`HA`/`NON_HA` on a managed service, `null` on a runtime), `created`/`lastUpdate`, `activeAppVersion{status, source: CLI                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | GIT                                                                                                                       | GITHUB | GITLAB | GUI | NONE, created, lastUpdate, githubIntegration, gitlabIntegration, publicGitSource}`, `currentAutoscaling`(the platform-filled envelope: cores 1–3, RAM 0.25–6 GB, disk 1–100 GB, containers 1–3 on a default runtime). A never-deployed runtime carries an`ACTIVE`app version with`source: NONE` (`z3-eval`'s `s3git1`). The envelope is deliberately not shown: the dashboard shows the allocation, and a range read as a figure. | `__fixtures__/z3-eval.service-stack.json` (captured 2026-09-04); the GUI's `service-stack-info-chips` template |
| **Six of the throwaway account's eight projects hold nothing but a zcp**; `Acme Docs - stage` holds `app` (`alpine/go@1.22`, public on 8080) and `db` (`postgresql:single@16`, ports 5432/6432) and no zcp, `Acme Docs - production` the same pair with `app` `READY_TO_DEPLOY`, `mate-gitea` a `web` (`ubuntu@26.04`, public 2222/3000), a `volume` and a `db`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | `GET /project/{id}/service-stack` over all eight                                                                          |
| **The rebuilt card at 1786 × 1000, dark, panel 507 px wide**: the zcp card is 161 px tall (name line, meta line, one route, the strip) against 204 px for the first attempt's boxed-tile version; the strip reads `1 container · Cores 2 · RAM 2.63 GB · Disk 2 GB` with fills 2 %, 19 %, 8 %.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | `getBoundingClientRect` and the strip's `data-zerops-service-metric` cells through `agent-browser eval`                   |

| Fact                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | Where                                                                             |
| --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **Before: the git toolbar strip (`.chat-composer-context-strip`) appeared at 322 ms and stayed until 3296 ms**, when Fen's socket connected and the Mate became known; the header showed `header-mate` only from 3296 ms. The composer moved twice per reload.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | probe on `/a5c9ebd6…/fff9217a…`                                                   |
| **Before: `zeropsMatesAtom` was published empty at ~218 ms** — the render in which the session settles still holds an empty list with `isLoading` false — and the cache went to `{}` (2 bytes), so even a cached identity was taken back for 430 ms.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | same probe, second reload                                                         |
| **After: `header-mate` from 0 ms, the strip never, the sync drawer never, the roster's empty state never**; the cache holds 277 bytes for three Mates. Two reloads: `0ms strip:false … headerMate:true cache:277` through `563ms mates:3`, then only the sync spinner.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | same probe, two reloads                                                           |
| **The sync spinner shows in the header slot for 40–480 ms** (1123–1164 ms; 1030–1509 ms on the second reload) while the thread catches up; the slot is 16 × 16 at x = 1011, y = 18 and is there whether or not it spins — nothing below it moves. `[data-thread-sync-drawer]` no longer exists.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | `[data-thread-sync-slot]`, `[data-thread-sync-indicator]`                         |
| **The composer glass is plain with the with-context class on**: `.chat-composer-glass-shell` carries `chat-composer-glass-shell-with-context` (ChatView still asks for a strip) while `::before` computes `clip-path: none`, `border-radius: 22px` — no shoulders under the corners.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | `getComputedStyle(shell, "::before")`                                             |
| **Server: the preview folds in per completed message and survives a summary refresh.** After the user's message the row holds `{"role":"user","text":"please do the thing",…}`; a streaming assistant delta leaves it; the completion with no text of its own reads the projected row and the preview becomes `{"role":"assistant","text":"working on it","createdAt":"…03.000Z"}`; five later refresh events keep it. Migration 045 backfills `thread-spoken` from its newest user/assistant message (`## Done\n\nThe thing is done.` → `Done The thing is done.`) and leaves `thread-quiet` null. | `ProjectionPipeline.test.ts`, `045_ProjectionThreadsLatestMessagePreview.test.ts` |
| **Tests**: 15 (`messagePreview`), 51 web (identities, cache, row, activity), 57 server (pipeline, repositories, snapshot query, migrations 024 + 045) pass; server, contracts, shared, client-runtime and web typecheck clean on the touched files; the token, exception (`no-infinite-motion` reconciled — the spinner is `ui/spinner`), manifest and zone guards pass.                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |                                                                                   |

## 0.4.0 released; zcp v9.166.0 pins it — 2026-09-06

The Mates release. Tag `v0.4.0` on `zeropsio/mate` (1b0987da5, main fast-forwarded from `zerops/groups-tools-environments`, 106 commits) → the release workflow (run 34003392956, 10 minutes to the bundle, 25 to the desktop legs) → `zerops-mate-0.4.0.tgz` (20,706,313 B, SHA-256 `5bf292fc…54a4f`, computed locally from the downloaded asset and equal to the release's `SHA256SUMS`) → zcp pin commit 519106a5 (`PinnedVersion`, `PinnedSHA256`, the four expectations in `TestInstallArgs_UsesPinnedReleaseAsset`; the serve golden reproduced by `scripts/mate-pin.sh` unchanged) → zcp `v9.166.0`, published 01:35:26 UTC (run 34004011461, test · lint · four builds green).

| Fact                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | Evidence                                                          |
| -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **CI runs only on `main` and pull requests**, so the release commit was the branch's first CI run: it failed on five tests behind their subjects — the creation plan's tag list (the `mate` tag written at birth), the boot shell's 44 × 52 box, the two-part lockup's marker, the header's sync slot, a retired word in a comment — none of them product defects. Fixed in 660f4600e and 62415ba83, whose CI run (34004233915) is green; the release artifact never depended on them (the workflow builds and packs, it does not run the suites). | `gh run view 34003393003 --log-failed`, `gh run view 34003682642` |
| **zcp's `make release` refuses a dirty tree and runs `go test ./... -short`, which needs the knowledge corpus pulled** (`make sync`; the guides are gitignored and CI pulls them the same way — `TestStore_GuidesEmbedded` fails on a stale checkout). With another session's uncommitted spec edit in the tree, its steps were run by hand: the suite (green after the sync), `git tag -a v9.166.0`, `git push origin HEAD v9.166.0`.                                                                                                             | `Makefile` `_release`, `/tmp/zcp-test.log`                        |
| **Not observed from this machine: the containers installing 0.4.0.** No ssh route to a container (`zcp` does not resolve without the project VPN). By the previous release's observation (0.3.1, 2026-09-05) a container picks the new zcp up at boot through `install.sh`, and `zcp init` then installs the pinned mate; a hand-pushed `-dev.` build is kept unless `zcp mate update --force`. Until then the environments run 0.3.1 and the client falls back to the title for a Mate's subject.                                                 | this ledger, 2026-09-05                                           |

## Cloning a Mate and Gitea CI/CD — read-only probes — 2026-09-06

Read with the owner's `zcli` token against the Onboarding org; nothing was created. The reasoning they back is `../zcp/plans/z3-new-mate-and-gitea-cicd-research-2026-09-06.md`.

| Fact                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | Evidence                                                                                             |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **Every dev-container import mints its own integration token.** `GET /client/{id}/integration-token/list` (the bare `…/integration-token` GET is `methodNotAllowed`) lists seven tokens named `zcp-<project name>`, each `roleCode: NO_ACCESS` on the org, `canCreateProjects: false` and `ADMIN` on exactly one project; their `created` stamps match the environment creations to the second. A token copied from one environment into another would make that zcp bind to the _source_ project — `auth.discoverProject` takes the single project the token sees. | `integration-token/list` read; `../zcp/internal/auth/auth.go`                                        |
| **The export's project vault still carries `ZCP_API_KEY` in clear**, while the zcp service's vault comes back `REDACTED` + `sensitive: true` — except the non-sensitive `ZCP_AGENT_OAUTH_CLAUDE_CODE` flag, also in clear.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | export of `Mx0EAKnDTm2tTnA40uwpFw`, key names and redaction flags only                               |
| **The live Gitea (`mate-gitea`, `web-1d76-3000.prg1.zerops.app`) answers `1.27.2`, has no user yet and no `runner` service**; its API sends no `Access-Control-Allow-Origin` (preflight 405), so the browser client cannot call it cross-origin — the two probes `deriveGiteaState` takes have to be fetched server-side or the recipe needs `[cors]`.                                                                                                                                                                                                              | `GET /api/v1/version`, `/api/v1/users/search?q=`, an `Origin:` probe; `GET /project/…/service-stack` |
| **`Acme Docs - production` still holds `app` at `READY_TO_DEPLOY`** — the 09-05 clone's `buildFromGit` build failed and nothing was deployed since.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | `GET /project/5tdxFTNGR7K1VXI74OBqlg/service-stack`                                                  |

## Gitea credentials without a human — 2026-09-06

One live read against the Onboarding org (nothing created) plus source reads of `go-gitea/gitea` at
tag `v1.27.2` and `zeropsio/recipe-gitea@main`. Together they say a Gitea instance can mint its own
admin credential and hand it to the client, which is what "a new Mate comes with Gitea already set
up" needs. The design is `../zcp/plans/z3-new-mate-and-gitea-cicd-research-2026-09-06.md` §3.8.

| Fact                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | Evidence                                                                                             |
| ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **`GET /service-stack/{id}/user-data` returns sensitive values in clear** to a token with the owner's role — on Gitea's `web` service, `SECRET_KEY`, `INTERNAL_TOKEN`, `JWT_SECRET`, `LFS_JWT_SECRET` and `DB_PASSWORD` all came back readable, each flagged `sensitive: true`. So a credential a container mints for itself is readable by the account that owns it, with the token mate already holds. Note the contrast with `GET /project/{id}/export`, which redacts service vault entries — `user-data` is the read to use, the export is not.                                                                                                                         | `GET /service-stack/YI8dOA9KSTqQu4mNBADwYQ/user-data?limit=40`, 35 entries, key names and flags only |
| **The recipe already uses that channel for its own secrets.** `init.sh` generates `JWT_SECRET`, `LFS_JWT_SECRET`, `SECRET_KEY` and `INTERNAL_TOKEN` with the Gitea binary and writes each back with `zsc setEnv --sensitive <key> -` (value on stdin, because a generated value can begin with `-`); `start.sh` exits until all four are present and the platform restarts it with them set. Gitea's admin user and API token are the same shape and the only two the recipe does not yet make for itself.                                                                                                                                                                   | `zeropsio/recipe-gitea@main`: `init.sh`, `start.sh`                                                  |
| **Gitea 1.27.2 can bootstrap its own admin non-interactively.** `gitea migrate` exists for exactly this ("so that you can run `gitea admin create user` before starting the server"): `initDB` calls `db.InitEngine` and does not migrate, `migrate` calls `InitEngineWithMigration`. `admin user create --random-password --access-token` then prints `generated random password is '…'` and `Access token was successfully created... <token>`, and `--access-token-scopes` defaults to `all` (1.27.2 rejects a token with no permission scope, so the scope-less token of #33474 is not reachable). Captured into shell variables, neither value reaches a log or `argv`. | `cmd/migrate.go`, `cmd/helper.go`, `cmd/admin_user_create.go` at `v1.27.2`                           |
| **Gitea ignores the username in HTTP basic auth whenever the password is a token.** `parseAuthBasic` sets `authToken = passwd` for any non-empty password, and `VerifyAuthToken` resolves the user from `token.UID` without ever comparing the username; the username is only used by the password-login fallback a valid token never reaches. zcp's credential helper (`username=oauth2`, token as password) therefore authenticates as the token's owner against Gitea unchanged.                                                                                                                                                                                          | `services/auth/basic.go` at `v1.27.2`                                                                |
| **A Gitea token cannot mint another token.** `/users/{username}/tokens` is guarded by `reqSelfOrAdmin() + reqBasicOrRevProxyAuth()`, so it needs HTTP basic auth — a site admin's basic auth can mint for any user. `/admin/users` (creating the user) is `tokenRequiresScopes(admin) + reqToken() + reqSiteAdmin()` and does accept an `all`-scoped token. A per-Mate Gitea identity therefore needs the admin **password** published beside the admin token.                                                                                                                                                                                                               | `routers/api/v1/api.go` lines 1080–1088 and 1802–1854 at `v1.27.2`                                   |

## Gitea, self-bootstrapped and driving production — run end to end — 2026-09-06

Built for real on the Onboarding org: the Gitea recipe patched to mint its own admin
(`zeropsio/recipe-gitea` branch `admin-bootstrap`), a fresh `mate-gitea` project imported from it,
every Mate on the account given its own Gitea credential, and a tag pushed through Gitea Actions
into a new production project. Two projects were deleted on the way (the empty original
`mate-gitea`, and a first attempt) with the owner's explicit go-ahead. The design is
`../zcp/plans/z3-new-mate-and-gitea-cicd-research-2026-09-06.md` §3.8–3.9.

| Fact                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | Evidence                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **The recipe can stand Gitea up with credentials and no human step: 174 s from import to published.** `admin-init.sh` runs `gitea migrate`, `gitea admin user create --admin --random-password --access-token`, and publishes `GITEA_ADMIN_PASSWORD` and `GITEA_ADMIN_TOKEN` with `zsc setEnv --sensitive`. Read back through `GET /service-stack/{id}/user-data`, the token authenticates as `mate` with `is_admin: true` and the password authenticates basic-auth routes.                  | live import of `recipe-gitea@admin-bootstrap`; watcher timings; `GET /api/v1/user`, `GET /api/v1/users/mate/tokens`                                                                                              |
| **Init commands run on every container start; `zsc execOnce` is what makes one run once.** What does _not_ re-run them is a failing start command: the platform re-runs the **start command alone**. On the first boot `start.sh` exits until the secrets `init.sh` just wrote have propagated, so anything placed in `initCommands` gets one look at an empty environment and is never reached again. Moving `admin-init.sh` to the start command is what made it work.                      | first run: `RUN.INIT COMMANDS` at 07:53:09, start retried 07:53:25 → 07:53:28 with no init header. Restart of a live service at 08:23:41: `RUN.INIT COMMANDS` ran again and `execOnce` short-circuited `init.sh` |
| **A service env write takes ~15 s to reach a newly started process, not ~6 s.** `init.sh` published at 07:53:10; `start.sh` still saw nothing at 07:53:25 and did at 07:53:28. The second run took four start-command retries over ~13 s.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | container logs, both runs                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| **`gitea actions generate-runner-token` is not a database command** — it posts to the running server on `localhost:3000`, so it cannot run before `gitea web` starts (`connection refused`). Under `set -e`, and placed before the publish step, it destroyed an admin password and token that already existed in the database and were never written anywhere. Registration tokens come from `POST /api/v1/admin/actions/runners/registration-token` instead (note the `/actions/` segment). | container log 08:05:20; the route at `routers/api/v1/api.go:1843-1846`                                                                                                                                           |
| **`buildFromGit: <url>@<branch>` is accepted on import**, which is how a recipe change can be proven without touching the published `main`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | import of `https://github.com/zeropsio/recipe-gitea@admin-bootstrap`; the platform's own export emits the same form                                                                                              |
| **Q-B1 answered live: Gitea ignores the username when the password is a token.** `git ls-remote` succeeded with `oauth2`, with the token's owner, and with `nobody`. zcp's credential helper needs no Gitea flavour.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | three `git ls-remote` runs against `mate/go-hello-world-app`                                                                                                                                                     |
| **A per-Mate `write:repository` token is exactly as confined as intended.** Fen's token cloned the repository and pushed a branch, and was refused by `GET /api/v1/user` and `GET /api/v1/admin/actions/runners` with 403 — API scope categories gate those, repository permissions gate git.                                                                                                                                                                                                 | clone + push + two 403s                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| **The reconcile is idempotent on real Mates.** Four Mates (`Fen`, `Juno`, `Nova`, `zerops-mate10`) all read "never connected", each got a `mate/<bot>` token and `GITEA_URL`/`GITEA_TOKEN` written, and a second run with unchanged inputs reported all four "connected" and rewrote nothing.                                                                                                                                                                                                 | two runs of the §3.9 reconcile                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| **Re-minting works and leaves the old token alive.** Deleting `GITEA_ADMIN_TOKEN` and restarting took the "user exists" branch, changed the password and minted `automation-<epoch>`; both new values authenticate. Gitea's CLI cannot delete an access token, so the replaced one stays valid until removed by hand — rotation after a leak needs that extra step.                                                                                                                           | restart at 08:23:42; `GET /users/mate/tokens` lists both                                                                                                                                                         |
| **The whole CI path works: tag to production in about a minute.** `git push origin v1.0.0` → Gitea Actions run #1 on a Zerops-hosted runner → `zcli push --service-id --setup prod` with a project-scoped `ZEROPS_TOKEN` → production `ACTIVE`, serving `{"type":"go","greeting":"Hello from Zerops!","status":{"database":"OK"}}`. The runner addon went from import to one online runner in 100 s.                                                                                          | action run, `stack.build`, the app's own response                                                                                                                                                                |
| **`zeropsSetup` without `buildFromGit` is rejected** — `projectImportInvalidParameter`, _"app.buildFromGit: parameter is required for use of pipelineConfig"_. A pipeline-first production service takes `startWithoutCode: true` and no setup name; the setup arrives with the first `zcli push --setup`.                                                                                                                                                                                    | the failed prod import                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| **`enableSubdomainAccess: true` does not take on a `startWithoutCode` service.** The service came up with `subdomainAccess: false` and answered 502 on its derived host until `PUT /service-stack/{id}/enable-subdomain-access`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | service read + the fix                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| **The service env read returns cross-service references unresolved.** `GITEA_DOMAIN` comes back as the literal `web-${zeropsSubdomainHost}-3000.prg1.zerops.app`; a client needs `zeropsSubdomainHost` and `publicZone` off the project to build a URL.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | `GET /service-stack/{id}/user-data`                                                                                                                                                                              |

## The Star Wars TODO group, deployed from Gitea — 2026-09-06

Wired after the end-to-end run, when it turned out that run had built its own group and left Fen's
untouched. Same pipeline, second group.

| Fact                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | Evidence                                                        |
| ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **`Acme Docs - production` had never deployed.** It had held `app` at `READY_TO_DEPLOY` since the failed clone of 09-05. Its own Gitea repository (`mate/acme-docs`), its own integration token (`ADMIN` on that project, `NO_ACCESS` on the org), the two repository secrets and the tag workflow took it to `ACTIVE`, serving `{"type":"go","greeting":"Hello from Zerops!","status":{"database":"OK"}}`. | Gitea Actions runs on `v1.0.0` and `v1.0.1`; the app's response |
| **Public access cannot be enabled before a service has code.** `PUT /service-stack/{id}/enable-subdomain-access` returns 400 on a `startWithoutCode` service that has never deployed, and 200 once it has. It is a post-deploy call, and `enableSubdomainAccess: true` in the import does not survive to that point either.                                                                                 | both attempts on `Acme Docs - production`                       |
| **The workflow does not need the runner image to ship zcli.** `curl -L https://zerops.io/zcli/install.sh \| sh` then `"$HOME/.local/bin/zcli" push` deploys just as well as the `zcli` the runner recipe installs on `PATH`; both were run live. `$HOME`, not `/root` — host-mode jobs run as the `zerops` user, so a GitLab-style `image:` key and `/root/.local/bin` are both wrong on this runner.       | `acme-docs` runs 2 and 3                                        |
| **The group's environments are not the same application.** `acme-docs-dev` (Fen) runs `alpine/nodejs@22` with no git source; stage and production run `alpine/go@1.22` from `go-hello-world-app`. The dev environment a Mate works in has drifted from what production ships — the lossy-clone failure sitting in a live account, not a hypothetical.                                                       | project exports of all three                                    |
