## Fen's own app shipped by Gitea, and a service deleted for no reason — 2026-09-06

Fen was told where its Gitea credential was and which repository was its own, looked before it wrote,
reported that the remote held an unrelated Go project, and force-pushed its Node tree over it. What
followed repaired the pipeline around the application that actually exists.

| Fact                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | Evidence                                                                                                   |
| ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **`run.base` in `zerops.yaml` sets a service's runtime; the service type is only its starting value.** The docs say it plainly — _"Sets the base technology for the runtime environment. If not specified, the current version is maintained."_ So a Go-typed service takes a Node application by deploying a `zerops.yaml` whose `run.base` is `alpine/nodejs@22`. **Deleting and re-importing the service to change its runtime is unnecessary**, and it costs the service id, which every repository secret referencing `ZEROPS_PROD_SERVICE_ID` then has to be repaired against. Done the wrong way here and corrected by the owner. | `zerops-yaml/specification` §Runtime Configuration → base; the needless delete of `cSkc29j5Tq6YBkMn3wY8Jg` |
| **A force-push removes workflow files; repository secrets survive it.** Fen's push replaced `main` and took `.gitea/workflows/deploy-prod.yaml` with it, while `ZEROPS_TOKEN` and `ZEROPS_PROD_SERVICE_ID` were untouched — they are repository settings, not tree contents. CI wiring therefore has to be re-landed after any history rewrite, and the secrets do not.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | `GET /repos/mate/acme-docs/contents/.gitea/workflows` 404 with both secrets still listed                   |
| **The Star Wars TODO group is now coherent end to end.** `acme-docs` holds Fen's Node/Express app, production runs `nodejs@22` on port 3000, and tag `v2.0.0` deployed it through Gitea Actions in 68 s. It serves _Galactic Todo Registry_ at `app-2fdc-3000.prg1.zerops.app`. The dev/prod application mismatch recorded earlier today is resolved in favour of the application a Mate actually develops.                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | the action run, the service read, the page title                                                           |
| **The Go sample was not lost by the force-push.** It remains in `mate/go-hello-world-app` with its own working tag pipeline, so nothing needed preserving out of `acme-docs`'s unreachable history.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | the other repository, still deploying `hello-go - production`                                              |

## A second account, from empty, by API — and what a browser cannot read — 2026-09-06

The owner opened a brand new Zerops account (`ales+mate@zerops.io`, org `Mate`, client
`m1VrZPJlSnAmnYAvfuEZEg`) and asked to see the flow from nothing. Every timing below is that
account's, not the Onboarding org's, so they are second observations rather than repeats.

| Fact                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | Evidence                                                                                          |
| -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **A user access token reads `REDACTED` where an integration token reads the value.** `GET /service-stack/{id}/user-data` answered `content: "REDACTED"` (8 characters) for `GITEA_ADMIN_PASSWORD` and `GITEA_ADMIN_TOKEN` under the token `POST /auth/login` returns, while `sensitive: true` was set on both. The same read with an integration token returned 12 and 40 characters in clear. The earlier "sensitive values come back in clear" observation was made with a zcli token and does not hold for a browser session. | both reads of `yQo96LxlR0OYqUvguJLAxg`; the 401 that followed from using `"REDACTED"` as a bearer |
| **A project-scoped integration token is enough.** `roleCode: NO_ACCESS` at the org with `projects: [{projectId: <gitea>, roleCode: "ADMIN"}]` reads that project's sensitive service env in clear and nothing else. This is the shape the client should mint when it stands Gitea up (B-8): the account's Gitea key, confined to the Gitea project, rather than an org-wide token in a browser.                                                                                                                                  | `gitea-admin-read` token against the same service                                                 |
| **Gitea self-bootstrapped in 184 s on a cold account**, against ~175 s measured on the Onboarding org: import to `GITEA_ADMIN_PASSWORD` and `GITEA_ADMIN_TOKEN` published. `GITEA_ADMIN_USERNAME` lands at 76 s (it is baked into `run.envVariables`); the two secrets at 184 s, together, as `admin-init.sh` publishes them.                                                                                                                                                                                                    | the poll log, 11 s cadence                                                                        |
| **One runner was online 86 s after the addon import**, against ~100 s before. `minContainers: 1` with `maxContainers: 1`; the published recipe ships 3.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | `GET /admin/actions/runners`                                                                      |
| **The runner recipe already installs zcli** to `/usr/local/bin/zcli` in `prepareCommands`. A workflow needs no `curl … install.sh` step and no `$HOME/.local/bin` path — plain `zcli` resolves. The runbook's install step is redundant on this runner.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | `zerops.yaml` setup `runner` in `zeropsio/recipe-gitea`                                           |
| **The published runner import needs no `zeropsSetup`.** Hostname `runner` selects the setup named `runner`; the platform's own `zerops-runner-import.yaml` carries none. The runbook's explicit `zeropsSetup: runner` is harmless but unnecessary.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | `zerops-runner-import.yaml`, and the import that built the right setup without it                 |
| **`createToolProject` in the fork already does the Gitea half of the runbook** — create tagged project, read `publicZone` for the region, import `buildGiteaImportYaml(region)`. The run used its exact YAML, so what stood Gitea up here is what the "Add Gitea" button sends.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | `packages/client-runtime/src/zerops/api.ts`, `giteaRecipe.ts`                                     |
| **A zcp container was `ACTIVE` 75 s after `PUT /first-class-recipe/development-container`.**                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | `Aurora - dev` `g6tsEJyhRPCFBv9tXGn3og`, service `O5pBJKpUQyA55z42pYtagw`                         |
| **The empty projects screen now answers with an invitation.** A fresh account showed a "Tools" heading with nothing under it, a bare "+ Add Gitea" and the sentence "No projects in this account yet." It now carries one card — the mark's own face at rest, what a Mate is, and `New project` — with the header's duplicate create button withheld while it is up.                                                                                                                                                             | `1d6eb2f4c`; verified at 1786 and 1280, light and dark                                            |

## Aurora: empty account to production, and the two gaps it exposed — 2026-09-06

The whole flow run on the fresh account, with the owner doing one thing (authorizing Claude Code) and
the brief delivered through the client itself. `Aurora` — a kudos wall for an office screen — is live
at `app-2feb-3000.prg1.zerops.app`, deployed by Gitea Actions from tag `v1.0.0`.

| Fact                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | Evidence                                                      |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **A Mate sees exactly one project, and it shows.** Told to build an application and ship it, Iris read its project, found only its own `zcp`, concluded there was nowhere to run anything, and provisioned `nodejs@22` + `postgresql:single@18` of its own. It never learned that its group had a production environment — group membership is a tag, and the container's token is `NO_ACCESS` with `ADMIN` on one project. The pipeline worked only because the production service id was in a repository secret a person put there. | the conversation; the dev project's new `app` and `db`        |
| **The fix is the same channel as the credential.** `MATE_GROUP` and `MATE_ENVIRONMENTS` (role, name, project id, services) written onto the Mate's `zcp` by the client, which is the only actor that can read the group tags. No credential travels with them: knowing production exists is not permission to deploy into it.                                                                                                                                                                                                         | `964c5849a`; written live onto `O5pBJKpUQyA55z42pYtagw`       |
| **A browser cannot read Gitea's API cross-origin without the recipe change.** `fetch('…/api/v1/version')` from a page on `localhost` fails with `Failed to fetch` against a `main`-built instance and answers `{"version":"1.27.2"}` against one built from the `cors` branch; `users/search` likewise. So `deriveGiteaState`'s probe has never once run in the product.                                                                                                                                                              | the two instances, side by side; `recipe-gitea` PR #3, merged |
| **Tag to production `ACTIVE` was ~70 s** on this account (`v1.0.0` at 1182 s into the watch, production `ACTIVE` by 1251 s), against ~68 s on the Onboarding org.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | the Gitea Actions run and the service read                    |
| **`enable-subdomain-access` still 400s before the first deploy and 200s after** — third observation, now on a service the CI deployed rather than a person.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | the 400 mid-run and the 200 after `success`                   |
| **The Mate delivered its own brief through the client.** The composer is a Lexical editor: `Input.insertText` does not reach it and neither does `Control+a`; selecting its contents with the Selection API and pressing Backspace clears it, and Playwright's `fill` inserts at the caret. Worth knowing for any future audit that has to type into a Mate.                                                                                                                                                                          | the audit browser session                                     |

## A Mate's group is its token — 2026-09-06

The env-var copy written earlier today lasted about an hour. What replaced it is the answer to the
question the copy was working around: an agent could not see its group because its **token** reached
one project, not because nobody had told it.

| Fact                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | Evidence                                                                                  |
| ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **`READ_ONLY` is a real project role.** The platform's own validation names the set: `OWNER, ADMIN, BASIC_USER, READ_ONLY, NO_ACCESS`. Anything else is a 400 that helpfully enumerates them.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | `projects[0].roleCode.errors` on seven rejected values                                    |
| **A token with `ADMIN` on one project and `READ_ONLY` on another reads the second and cannot change it.** Own project 200, sibling project 200, sibling's service list 200 (`app`, `db`, `core`), `PUT /service-stack/{id}/restart` on the sibling **403**.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | a probe token against `Aurora - dev` and `Aurora - production`                            |
| **`PUT /client/{clientId}/integration-token/{tokenId}` rewrites an existing token's grants in place, and the token string does not change.** The same bearer went 200 → 403 → 200 on a sibling across two writes. So widening a container's reach needs no new token, no env write and **no restart**.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | the probe token, read between each write                                                  |
| **`PATCH` on that route is 405 and `PUT /integration-token/{id}` (no client prefix) is 404.** The client-scoped `PUT` is the only shape.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | both attempts                                                                             |
| **A dev container's token is `zcp-<project name>`** with a single `ADMIN` grant on its project. Identifying it needs both halves — the name alone breaks on a project rename, the grant alone is indistinguishable from a deploy token — and the grant test must be "grants ADMIN on this project", never "grants only it", or the widening loses its own token.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | `GET /client/{id}/integration-token/list` on the account: four tokens, three single-grant |
| **Proven end to end on Iris.** Its token widened to `READ_ONLY` on `Aurora - production`, `MATE_GROUP` and `MATE_ENVIRONMENTS` deleted from its environment, container restarted: it answers about its group from the platform, with no copy to go stale.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | `zcp-Aurora - dev` `rg6S0YTxT0Kl2kvhp1BJrw`; the conversation                             |
| **`READ_ONLY` redacts a sibling's sensitive values but not everything carrying a secret.** Production's `db` answers `password` and `superUserPassword` as `REDACTED`, and `connectionString` — which the platform does **not** mark sensitive — in clear, password included. Survivable rather than harmless: the host in that string is `db`, which resolves inside whichever project the reader is in, so from a Mate's container it points at the Mate's own database, and the credential is only usable on production's private network. The platform offers no finer grant than `READ_ONLY`, so the choice is sight with that leak or the blindness it replaced. | a scoped probe token against `Aurora - production`'s `db`                                 |
| **The boundary holds against an agent that actually pushes on it.** Told its group variables were gone, Iris read the production project and its services, confirmed `app_appVersionName="v1.0.0"` matched what it had shipped, then tried a reversible write there — refused with `insufficientPermissions` — proved the same write succeeds in its own project with a throwaway service, and deleted it. No `MATE_GROUP`, no `MATE_ENVIRONMENTS`, nothing but the token.                                                                                                                                                                                             | the conversation; both projects' service lists after                                      |
| **Open: the `zerops_*` MCP server did not come back for a session held open across a container restart.** Iris reported "the zerops MCP server just disconnected" and fell back to raw `zcli`/`curl`/`ssh`. Whether it reconnects on its own, or a held-open conversation needs re-establishing, is not yet measured.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | the conversation, after the restart                                                       |

## `buildFromGit` will not build from the account's own Gitea — 2026-09-07

Found while proving the recipe path in the UI. A stage tier whose `app` pointed at
`mate/aurora` on the account's own Gitea never built, and the discriminator says it is the host, not
the project and not the URL form.

| Fact                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | Evidence                                                                                 |
| ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **A `buildFromGit` at a `*.zerops.app` Gitea fails with `unexpected internal server error`.** Twice, on two services in one project — with and without the `.git` suffix the repository's own `clone_url` advertises.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | `stack.build FAILED` on `app` and `probeapp` in `Aurora - stage`                         |
| **The same project builds from GitHub.** A third service in it, sourced from `github.com/zeropsio/recipe-gitea`, failed with `The setup was not found.` — a precise error from a build that actually ran, because the hostname matched no setup in that repo's `zerops.yaml`. So the project, its import and the builder are all fine.                                                                                                               | `stack.build FAILED` on `ghprobe`, same project, same import mechanism                   |
| **The repository is anonymously clonable from outside.** `git ls-remote https://web-2fe9-3000.prg1.zerops.app/mate/aurora.git` returns `HEAD`, `refs/heads/main` and `refs/tags/v1.0.0` with no credentials, and the repo reports `private: false`.                                                                                                                                                                                                  | the `ls-remote`, and `GET /repos/mate/aurora`                                            |
| **Nothing on the intended path depends on it.** A recipe tier imports the shell — the services a project is made of, declared ready to deploy — and the CI/CD that shell sets up pushes the app in afterwards. `buildFromGit` at import time was a shortcut in a hand-written tier, not the design: the seeded recipes already source from `github.com/zerops-recipe-apps/*`, and production and stage deploy through `zcli push` from the workflow. | the `v1.0.0` deploy, which used `zcli push --setup app` and worked; `recipeStoreSeed.ts` |
| **Cause not established from here.** The error is server-side and opaque; whether the builder cannot reach a `zerops.app` subdomain from the build network or chokes on something Gitea serves is not distinguishable without platform logs.                                                                                                                                                                                                         | —                                                                                        |

## 0.6.0 released and installed by zcp v9.169.0 on a live container — 2026-09-07

The token-reach release, and the first one watched all the way onto a container. Order mattered
here: 0.6.0's reconcile widens a Mate's token to its whole group, which zcp before v9.168.0 read as
"two projects" and refused — so zcp shipped first, mate second, the pin third.

| Fact                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | Evidence                                                                        |
| ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **zcp v9.168.0 first, and the fix proved on the failure it was written for.** It carries `fix(auth): a container's identity is stated, not counted` (`f193b534`). Iris, restarted onto it, answered "Working again — reports project _Aurora - dev_ (`g6tsEJyhRPCFBv9tXGn3og`), same as before, with app/db/zcp all ACTIVE" — on the very token whose two-project reach had broken `zerops_*`. | the conversation, after the restart                                             |
| **mate v0.6.0 published**: tag object `fdc44bb551b3c4b2dca9e8df6ab0192e572d23fe` on commit `172c2b4486cb53fe584b7ce3e9a28fb7dd26ed86`, `zerops-mate-0.6.0.tgz` at 20 721 268 bytes, digest `b7c4206e32dbf4192fee284c66dc7be84950569c6b7ee64e1337b0d315b57cd1` matching the release's own `SHA256SUMS`.                                                                                         | `gh release view v0.6.0`                                                        |
| **zcp v9.169.0 pins that exact digest.** `PinnedVersion = "0.6.0"` and `PinnedSHA256 = b7c4206e…57cd1` at tag `v9.169.0`, commit `3b3a127e7c6d8f011f1be3b91a77eefbd8b7c080`, four assets published 06:14:48Z. Released from a clean clone of `origin/main` so another session's uncommitted `docs/spec-mate.md` and plan were never touched — `make release` ran normally after `make sync`.   | `raw.githubusercontent.com/.../v9.169.0/internal/mate/mate.go`; run 34089519423 |
| **A container boots the new zcp and installs the pinned mate — measured, not inferred.** The 0.5.0 entry could only assume this. Restart at 06:23:12Z, `ACTIVE` at 06:24:02Z (50 s); on the container, `zcp version` → `zcp v9.169.0 (3b3a127e7c6d8f011f1be3b91a77eefbd8b7c080, 2026-09-07T06:13:47Z)` and `~/.zcp/mate/current/node_modules/.bin/mate --version` → `mate v0.6.0`.             | Iris, in the `zcp` service of `Aurora - dev`                                    |
| **The client noticed the skew itself and offered the fix.** With a 0.6.0 web client against a 0.5.x server it showed "Server versions differ … follows the release pinned by zcp" with a _Restart to install_ button, confirmed through "Running threads stop. Restart now?". The restart above was that button, and the notice was gone on the next read.                                     | the notice, before and after                                                    |
| **`zcp --version` is not a flag — it starts the server.** It falls through to `server run` and prints connect/disconnect logs. `zcp version` is the subcommand that answers.                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | both outputs, verbatim                                                          |
| **The flaky mate test was flaky.** `nativeReviewDiffHighlighter > keeps grammar state across inline comment rows` failed on a docs-only commit that cannot touch it, passed on the commit before, passed 3/3 locally, and passed on re-run.                                                                                                                                                    | run 34088942746, green after re-run                                             |

## What a container's env reads back, and which agent flag survives it — 2026-09-07

Asked because "a new Mate should arrive already authorized" needs a source for the group's agents.
Measured against Aurora's dev zcp (`O5pBJKpUQyA55z42pYtagw`), `GET /service-stack/{id}/env`, 19 records.

| Fact                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | Impact                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **Every `envSecrets` entry reads back `REDACTED`.** `ZCP_AGENTS`, `ZCP_AGENT_AUTH_TYPE_CLAUDE_CODE`, `ZCP_MATE_ENABLED`, `ZCP_VSCODE`, `ZCP_VSCODE_AUTH_ENABLED`, `VSCODE_PASSWORD` and `GITEA_TOKEN` all came back as the literal `REDACTED`, not their values. | The client cannot read back a secret it did not write, so a sibling's agent _selection_ is not a readable source for anything.                                                                                                |
| **`ZCP_AGENT_OAUTH_CLAUDE_CODE` reads back `true`.** Written non-sensitive by `zcp agent mark-oauth` precisely so the GUI's flag path can see it (`spec-mate.md` §8.1).                                                                                          | The one readable, per-agent signal — and the better one: it says which agents an environment is _signed in with_, not which ones somebody once picked. It is what `agentSelection.ts` derives a new Mate's `ZCP_AGENTS` from. |
| **The agent credential is not in the env at all.** It is a file in the container (`~/.claude/.credentials.json`), whose presence the mate server reports and whose contents it never does; MC-11 forbids an agent token in the import document.                  | "A new Mate arrives already authorized" is **not reachable from the browser** — not by copying a token and not by copying env. It needs a place the account can store an agent credential, which does not exist today.        |
| **`GITEA_REPO` and `GITEA_URL` are readable; `GITEA_TOKEN` is not.** Present on the dev zcp alongside the agent flags.                                                                                                                                           | The Gitea half of the flow already leaves readable traces on a container, which is where a "which repo is this Mate pushing to" read would come from.                                                                         |
| **Only the dev environment has a zcp.** Aurora's stage and production `service-stack` lists carry none, so a group's authorized set comes from its Mates alone.                                                                                                  | `readGroupAgents` reads the environments that have a container and unions them; a group with no Mate yet answers empty, which omits `ZCP_AGENTS` and offers every agent.                                                      |

## Two demo projects built end to end from an empty account — 2026-09-07

The investor demo, built by driving the real client in a real browser against a wiped account
(`ales+mate@zerops.io`, org `m1VrZPJlSnAmnYAvfuEZEg`). Statuspage: one Mate, one production.
Shortlink: two Mates, a stage, a production. Both on a self-hosted Gitea with a tag-triggered
deploy. Four public URLs answered 200 at the end.

| Fact                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | Impact                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **Agent authorization does not depend on the page that started it.** The tab was navigated away mid-flow, to the project list and into another dialog; on returning, all five steps (Start / Initialize session / Authorize in browser / Verify code / Complete) read COMPLETE and `ZCP_AGENT_OAUTH_CLAUDE_CODE` was `true` on the container. The dialog reports on the container, it does not host the flow. | A sign-in cannot be lost by a misclick or a second tab, and nothing needs to warn the user to stay put. Measured by accident, which is the only way this gets measured.                   |
| **A cloned environment's runtime arrives `READY_TO_DEPLOY`, with no code, whatever the source was built by.** Both `Shortlink - stage` and `Shortlink - production` came up with `app` empty. The dialog's "will need a deploy" note is keyed on `builtFromGit`, which was empty for a service deployed by `zerops_deploy`, so no note appeared — and the service still needed a deploy.                      | The warning answers "was this built from git", but the question the user has is "will this run when it comes up". Those came apart here, silently. The clone is a shape, never a copy.    |
| **A production shipped only by a git tag, twice.** `mate/statuspage` `v1.0.1` and `mate/shortlink` `v1.0.0` each ran a Gitea Actions workflow to `success` and put a live app behind a Zerops subdomain, with a per-project integration token (`NO_ACCESS` + one project at `ADMIN`) as the only credential in the CI.                                                                                        | The delivery story holds without anyone touching the platform GUI: the tag is the deploy.                                                                                                 |
| **Production keeps its own database.** Shortlink production came up with an empty links table while dev held Cleo's test data, and had to be seeded through its own form.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | Confirms a clone carries services, not their contents — worth saying out loud in a demo, since an empty production reads as a broken one.                                                 |
| **Two client bugs surfaced only by adding the _second_ of something.** Adding a second Mate proposed the first one's environment name, and the creation handoff was spent on a send that never happened (`2637a7897`, `59525cc83`). Both paths are exercised constantly with N=1 and were correct there.                                                                                                      | The group model's whole point is N Mates per project; N=1 is the case the code was written against and N=2 is the case it was not. Worth reaching for whenever a group feature is tested. |

## Native platform data and receiver replacement — 2026-09-07

Read-only Node 24.19.0 probes against `https://api.app-prg1.zerops.io/api/rest/public`,
using the existing local account credential without printing or changing it.
No browser, container command, platform mutation or deployment was performed.
Two organizations were inspected to select a nonempty accessible scope. Socket
receiver IDs were fresh UUIDs owned by the probe and sockets were closed afterward.
The final run began at 12:58:00 UTC and replaced its receiver at 12:59:26 UTC.

Reproduction protocol (authenticated requests; substitute accessible IDs):

1. `GET /user/info`; `POST /web-socket/login` with `{ "token": "<account-token>" }`;
   connect to `wss://api.app-prg1.zerops.io/api/rest/public/web-socket/<receiver>/<webSocketToken>`.
2. `POST /{service-stack|process|project}/search`, body
   `{ "search": [{ "name": "clientId", "operator": "eq", "value": "<org>" }],
"limit": 100, "receiverId": "<receiver>", "subscriptionName": "<distinct-name>",
"wsOutputType": "listStream" }`; separately register `updateStream` with
   `disableOutput: true`. Second receiver registered update before list.
3. Current metrics: `POST /current-stats/group-by-search` with clientId/projectId
   equality filters, `groupBy: "serviceStackId"`, receiverId and subscriptionName.
   Two distinct names for the same query were registered simultaneously. The first
   phase also included `billingEnabled is true`.
4. History: `POST /stats-history/group-by-search` with clientId/projectId equality
   filters, `groupBy: "serviceStackId"`, `timeGroupBy: "1m"`, `limit: 10`,
   `timeZone: "Europe/Prague"`, receiverId and subscriptionName.
5. Send JSON `{ "type": "ping" }` every 15 seconds. Observe, close the owned socket,
   exchange a new websocket token, create a fresh receiver, register interests
   again and observe another 85 seconds. An earlier run observed 180 seconds.

| Measured fact                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | Scope and consequence                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Login and all six entity registrations returned 200; greeting was `SocketSuccess`, heartbeat returned `pong`. List responses contain `items`, `limit`, `offset`, `totalHits`; update registration returned `success`.                                | Registration responses are usable inputs. This does not establish an atomic snapshot/stream boundary.                                                                                                                                        |
| ServiceStack, Process and Project search rows contain numeric `_version`. Process rows additionally contain `parentId`, `rootId` and numeric `sequence`; sampled parent/root values were null.                                                       | Retain metadata; comparability, monotonicity and causal meaning were not tested. `_version` is not evidence of a replay cursor.                                                                                                              |
| Both differently named current-stat subscriptions received `type: search` frames with `data.items` plus pagination metadata. History frames carried `data.update`, `timeGroupBy`, `groupBy`, `limit`.                                                | Actual multiplexed native telemetry was observed, not only a successful registration. Current and historical data need different decoders/reducers.                                                                                          |
| History with `billingEnabled is true` and `groupBy: serviceStackId` returned 400: `invalid filter/groupBy combination billingEnabled/serviceStackId`. Removing that filter returned 200 and native history updates.                                  | The current backend does not accept this legacy history request combination. Tested interval was `1m`; an initial `minute` spelling was corrected before isolating the filter failure.                                                       |
| After fresh receiver/token and explicit registration, current metrics and history arrived again. The final run received nine named frames total, including two Process updates, one Process membership add and one Project update after replacement. | Practical reconnect-by-replacement works for these subscriptions. The probe initiated no platform operations; these entity changes originated outside it. Same-receiver retention, replay, unsubscribe and registration TTL were not tested. |
| Native Process/Project `data.update` records also contain `_version`; Process membership carries separate `data.add`/`data.delete` IDs.                                                                                                              | Supports distinct entity and query-membership inputs. No ServiceStack transition occurred in these passive windows; service status transitions are separately recorded in the 2026-09-04 entry.                                              |
| Project search and push records contain `envList`; process records contain embedded user/project/service objects.                                                                                                                                    | Decode explicit owned fields; never put entire raw records in a broad projection or diagnostic log. Probe output preserved entity shapes only and pseudonymized telemetry fixtures.                                                          |
| The chosen service search returned 100 items, while process/project sample counts differed; history `limit: 10` returned more than ten total rows across grouped series.                                                                             | A request limit is not a universal total-row or full-inventory guarantee. History/group and entity pagination must have source-specific coverage semantics.                                                                                  |

This was a protocol probe, not the proposed production runtime or an integrated UI
test. It did not establish source ordering, monotonic `_version`, atomic handoff,
lossless reconnect, command idempotency, membership revocation delivery, same-name
replacement or metric bucket correction semantics. Native fields and envelopes
must not be generalized into those guarantees.

## Production data runtime against a disposable project — 2026-09-07

The G1 implementation was exercised through its public client-runtime facade against a newly
created LIGHT project and one `alpine/nodejs@22` service. The harness used the existing local account
credential without printing it. It did not use a browser or a mate server. After the proof, the exact
project deletion succeeded and its ID was absent from the immediately following project listing.

| Measured fact                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | Scope and consequence                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Project topology and the separately acquired current-metrics interest both reached `observing`; the native current-stat registration produced two samples. A topology list read and direct service read referenced the same canonical Service record.                                | The production adapter, reducer, interest runtime and Atom projections interoperate on live data. Current metrics remain optional and separately leased.                                                                                  |
| `restart-service` through the runtime command facade returned an accepted Process ID, and that Process was inserted into the same shared Process table used by stream observations.                                                                                                  | The measured `PUT /service-stack/{id}/restart` response has the project, service, action and process identity required by the command decoder. This proves response correlation for this command shape, not general platform idempotency. |
| Independent `zcli service stop` and `start` commands each produced a new Process observation in the already-running runtime, without a caller refresh.                                                                                                                               | Changes initiated outside the client converge through the native Process stream into the same model.                                                                                                                                      |
| Closing the harness-owned socket exposed `recovering`; resubscription reached `observing` on a second socket with a higher receiver epoch and a different receiver ID. Peak buffered ingress was 13 decoded observations and 13,473 raw bytes, then drained to zero with no discard. | Receiver replacement, generation fencing and re-registration work in the measured disconnect case. This does not prove replay or lossless delivery during the gap.                                                                        |

The disposable organization/project/service IDs are retained only in the gitignored proof ledger;
durable documentation intentionally omits them. The proof performed restart, stop and start only on
that disposable service, then removed the containing project.

## Production data runtime burst measurement — 2026-09-07

The G1 production protocol decoder, bounded ingress, reducer runtime and Atom projections were run
with Node v24.19.0 on a MacBook Pro `Mac17,6` with an Apple M5 Max (18 cores) and 64 GB memory. The
synthetic input used captured platform frame shapes; it did not contact the Zerops API.

| Measured fact                                                                                                                                                                                                        | Scope and consequence                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| 10,000 raw captured-shaped frames, each decoding to five entity observations, were admitted in 40 bursts of 250 frames against a stable population of 500 services.                                                  | The run exercised 50,000 decoded observations through production decoding and state publication. It measures this bounded workload, source shape and machine; it is not a universal latency guarantee. |
| Decode-to-Atom burst latency was p50 105.13 ms, p95 118.76 ms and maximum 119.97 ms. The target Service projection published 299 times; an unrelated Service projection published zero times.                        | Atom-family selection avoided unrelated publication during the measured hot stream. The numbers include each complete 250-frame burst rather than isolated map lookup time.                            |
| Ingress drained to zero. Peak retained ingress was 1,244 decoded events and 144,440 raw bytes, with zero discarded events. The final model retained 500 services, two read diagnostics and no projects or processes. | The exercised load stayed below the configured 2,048-event and 8 MiB account limits. Bytes were charged once per source frame; decoded observations each consumed an event slot.                       |
| Reported heap delta after forced collection was -2,530,152 bytes.                                                                                                                                                    | This single post-collection delta shows no retained growth for the stable-population run. Identity/query churn and longer-duration profiling remain separate evidence needs.                           |

The production web client was run at `localhost:5734` against the local server at
`localhost:13774`, with state isolated under the checkout's `.t3`. The existing
account session exposed two organizations and 26 projects. Browser automation was
explicitly authorized for this pass. Temporary development-only inspection hooks
used to isolate the startup and expiry paths were removed before validation.

| Measured fact                                                                                                   | Scope and consequence                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| The first integrated load remained on `Checking your Zerops projects…` although REST reads had completed.       | The runtime `Ref` contained 26 projects and 134 services, while the root Atom had returned to its initial value before React subscribed. The runtime now mounts that Atom for its account scope, and the React selection store reconciles once after installing subscriptions. Two deterministic regression tests preserve both ordering cases. |
| Refresh, both organizations, empty/nonempty inventory, project/group rows and two different Mate views loaded.  | The shared inventory projected the same projects into the project page and sidebar. Existing and newly connected conversations opened without the startup spinner returning.                                                                                                                                                                    |
| A populated Zerops panel showed live runtime and infrastructure rows, service detail and current usage.         | In a 12-second observed window the client exchanged 24 received and 24 sent WebSocket frames. Payload contents were not inspected or logged. The service's public page loaded in the side panel and its reload control succeeded.                                                                                                               |
| Four seconds of browser-level offline state showed a reconnecting state; restoring networking recovered.        | Within the following 15 seconds the same view again showed live topology without returning to inventory loading or an access error. This proves replacement recovery for the exercised client interruption, not replay or lossless delivery during the gap.                                                                                     |
| Freezing the page lifecycle for four seconds and restoring it preserved the live topology.                      | The active view recovered after foregrounding without a stale loader or access error. This is a short suspension experiment, not a long-duration browser-memory measurement.                                                                                                                                                                    |
| A controlled expiry trigger showed the access-expired recovery surface; `Try again` revalidated successfully.   | The alert disabled product content and offered retry/sign-out. The live 15-minute wall-clock interval was not waited out; the production expiry transition was invoked directly, then its normal revalidation path recovered after all scoped reads completed.                                                                                  |
| A service created by the UI was stopped and started externally through zCLI while the project page stayed open. | The page changed to failed/reconnecting after stop, then returned to the service's server version after start. The service is identified in the gitignored work ledger and remains pending explicit approval for destructive cleanup.                                                                                                           |
| Signing out in one tab locked both tabs immediately.                                                            | Neither tab retained the project view. The subsequent Zerops handover reached the account-authorized `Continue to Zerops Code` step; completing that app-token grant remains pending explicit approval.                                                                                                                                         |

Source order, replay, lossless reconnect and universal backend atomicity retain the
limits stated in the architecture contract. The final app-token grant and cleanup are
recorded as pending in the local programme status rather than treated as completed.

## Zerops auth surface — token kinds, roles, delegations and the door — 2026-09-15

Measured with the owner's personal zcli token against the Onboarding org (`y6tz5g4lQVaENpmlknyrRw`),
on throwaway LIGHT projects tagged `probe:auth-0915` (`probe-auth-a`, `probe-auth-b`, a Mate
`probe-auth-mate` on mate 0.10.0, and two projects created by probe tokens) and 22 probe integration
tokens — all deleted after. The platform's OpenAPI (`/api/rest/public/swagger/openapi.yml`, 194
paths) states allowed roles per operation; every row below was measured, not read. The reasoning it
backs is `../zcp/plans/research/zerops-auth-backbone-2026-09-15.md`.

| Fact                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | Evidence                                                                            |
| ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **There are three token kinds and two of them are "application tokens".** A session token (`POST /auth/login`), a personal token (`POST /user-token`, created only from a session — personal and integration tokens get `403 notAllowedForApplicationToken`) and an integration token. `GET /auth/info` refuses both application kinds with the same code.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | `/auth/info`, `POST /user-token` with each kind                                     |
| **An integration token is an org member with an identity of its own.** Each appears in `GET /client/{id}/user/list` as `token-<id lowercased>@zerops.io` with its org role; its project grants are ordinary `userRoles` entries on the project; `/user/info` answers with `id` = the token id and one `clientUserList` entry.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | `/user/info` with 10 probe tokens; member list before/after minting                 |
| **Integration tokens pass the Mate door.** On a 0.10.0 Mate, a token with `BASIC_USER` or `ADMIN` on the Mate's project, and an org-wide `BASIC_USER` token with no project grant, each got a grant at `POST /mate/api/auth/zerops-identity` (`label: Zerops BASIC_USER`) and, after the exchange, a session carrying `orchestration:operate terminal:operate exec:operate` (899 s). `READ_ONLY` tokens and a token for another project got `403 zerops_project_membership_required`. So the container's own `ZCP_API_KEY` (`ADMIN` on its project) is a Mate operator key. The exchange issues whatever subset is requested; these probes requested none, while the product clients request the five standard scopes, without `exec:operate`. | door + `/oauth/token` + `/api/auth/session` on `probe-auth-mate`                    |
| **A project override lowers an org role — the org OWNER's included.** An org-`ADMIN` token with `READ_ONLY` on project A was refused by the door and on every write there, and read A's secrets as `REDACTED`. The owner's own custom role set to `READ_ONLY` on B (`PUT /client-user/{id}/roles`) made the owner's env write on B `403`; restored with an empty list.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | probe token `admin-a-ro`; owner override on `probe-auth-b`                          |
| **What each role can do, measured:** tags and rename (`PUT /project/{id}`) need effective `OWNER`/`ADMIN`; env create/delete and service stop need `BASIC_USER`; **`zcli push` works with `BASIC_USER` on the project** (build `FINISHED`, app version `ACTIVE`) and fails `insufficientPermissions` with `READ_ONLY`; `READ_ONLY` gets a one-hour log-stream URL from `GET /project/{id}/log`. Closes Q-B2 of the 2026-09-06 plan.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | write matrix over 10 tokens; `zcli push` with `ZEROPS_TOKEN` and an isolated `HOME` |
| **A `BASIC_USER` project token can build an environment in its own project.** On a fresh `probe:basicimport-2026-09-15` project, a token with `BASIC_USER` on it and `NO_ACCESS` on the org imported a `nodejs@22` `startWithoutCode` service (to `ACTIVE`), re-imported it with `override: true`, set a plain and a sensitive service env var, restarted and deleted the service — all `200`; `PUT /project/{id}` with tags answered `403 insufficientPermissions`. `enable-subdomain-access` answered `400 serviceStackIsNotHttp` (no HTTP port on the probe service — a state refusal, not a permission one). So lowering a Mate's container token to `BASIC_USER` keeps bootstrap's platform calls.                                        | probe token, one service, deleted after; org back to 9 tokens / 10 projects         |
| **Application tokens read sensitive values in clear at `BASIC_USER` and above**, through `GET /service-stack/{id}/user-data`; `READ_ONLY` reads `REDACTED`. `GET /user-data/{id}/reveal` answers `notInSudoMode` to every application token, although `GET /auth/sudo` reports `inSudo: true` for all of them.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | a `PROBE_SECRET` envSecret on A, read by each token                                 |
| **A token cannot make a token without a delegation.** Every integration token, org `ADMIN` included, got `notAllowedForIntegrationTokenWithoutDelegation` on `POST /client/{id}/integration-token`; asking for more than its own role answers `roleLevelExceeded` first. Updating or regenerating itself and creating a delegation answer `notAllowedForIntegrationToken`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | three mint shapes per token                                                         |
| **A delegation is one use, exact shape, owned by the person who granted it.** A token holding one (`NO_ACCESS` + `canCreateProjects`) was refused a different shape, then minted the delegated shape once; the delegation list went 1 → 0 and a second mint was refused. The minted token's `createdByUser` is the delegating user. A **project-scoped** delegation works: a token with no access to B minted a token with `ADMIN` on B — and a narrower shape (`BASIC_USER` on B) was refused.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | delegation list read by the token between each step                                 |
| **Environment creation mints three things.** `PUT /project/{id}/first-class-recipe/development-container` with `createIntegrationToken: true` created a `zcp-<project>` token (`NO_ACCESS`, `ADMIN` on the project, owned by the caller), its pseudo-member, and a one-time delegation `NO_ACCESS` + `canCreateProjects` granted by the caller. All seven older `zcp-*` tokens on the org carry the same delegation: every Mate can create one project.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | token, member and delegation lists diffed around the import                         |
| **Only the project import creates a project for a token below `ADMIN`.** A `NO_ACCESS` + `canCreateProjects` token got `400 userNotFound` from `POST /client/{id}/project` and `200` from `POST /client/{id}/project/import`, where its pseudo-user became the project's `OWNER`. The zcp token whose delegation minted it cannot read the new project (`403`).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | both calls with a directly minted and a delegated token                             |
| **A project-`ADMIN` token rewrites its own project's group tags and name.** Adding `mate`, `mate:g:<another group>`, `mate:role:prod` and `mate:bot:Impostor` answered 200, as did a rename; restored after. Group membership is therefore self-asserted by any Mate.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | `PUT /project/{id}` with the `ADMIN`-on-A token                                     |
| **Every token sees the whole org's people and groups.** A `NO_ACCESS` token with no grant at all read `GET /client/{id}/user/list` (21 members, emails and roles) and `GET /client/{id}/project/tag/list` (every `mate:g:` id and group name). `NO_ACCESS`-org tokens get `403` on `GET /client/{id}/project` even for projects they are granted; below org `ADMIN`, the token list shows metadata (no values) of tokens made by the same user. `GET /client-user/{id}/roles` is `403` to a `READ_ONLY` token, though `GET /project/{id}` shows `userRoles` to it.                                                                                                                                                                             | read matrix over 10 tokens                                                          |
| **A token can name the person behind it.** It reads its own `GET /client/{id}/integration-token/{tokenId}` including `createdByUser`, which the member list resolves to a person. Once the token is deleted, processes and app versions keep `token-…@zerops.io` as `createdByUser` but the pseudo-member is gone, so the person can no longer be resolved from the platform.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | self-read; process and app-version reads after `DELETE`                             |
| **Revocation is immediate at the platform and not at the Mate.** A deleted token read `401` within 0.6 s; a Mate session minted from it stayed valid, a grant minted before the deletion was exchanged after it for a fresh 15-minute session, and only a new door call failed (`401 invalid_credential`). Regenerating a token kills the old value at once.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | timed sequence against `probe-auth-mate`                                            |
| **A release can be a promotion, run by whoever may deploy to production.** A token with `BASIC_USER` on stage and production only: `GET /app-version/{id}/app-code` on stage's active version (a pre-signed URL on `proxy.app-prg1.zerops.io`), `POST /service-stack/{prod}/app-version`, `PUT /app-version/{id}/upload` with the downloaded bytes, `PUT /app-version/{id}/build-and-deploy` with the `zerops.yaml` text and setup → production app version `ACTIVE`, attributed to the token. The download URL, its preflight and the API's preflight all answer `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`, so a browser can run it on its own session.                                                                                                | `probe-auth-a` → `probe-auth-b`                                                     |
| **Zerops is not an OIDC provider.** `.well-known/openid-configuration` and `oauth-authorization-server` on `app.zerops.io` return the web app's HTML (SPA fallback, as for any path); the API host closes TLS on them; `/api/rest/public/oauth/authorize` is `404`. The GitHub/GitLab routes are the consumer side (login, registration, repository access). No endpoint signals membership changes.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | `curl` of each; OpenAPI path list                                                   |
| **zcp's self-deploy leaves the Mate's key in the app's dev container.** `buildSSHCommand` runs `zcli login -- <ZCP_API_KEY>` over SSH in the source service before `zcli push`, so the project token is written into that container's zcli config, where the app's own dependencies and dev server run — the Mate's key is exposed to app code, not only to shells in the Mate. Code read, not measured.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | `../zcp/internal/ops/deploy_ssh.go:236`                                             |

## Gitea 1.27.2 as a mirror of Zerops roles — local lab and a Zerops-backed OIDC prototype — 2026-09-15

Measured on local Gitea 1.27.2 instances (darwin/arm64, SQLite, `gitea-runner` 3.5.0 in host mode),
with the source at tag `v1.27.2` for pointers. The OIDC prototype is a ~200-line provider that
verifies a real Zerops token on every authorization and emits `groups` computed from the Onboarding
org's live roles and `mate:g:` tags; it backed a second local Gitea. Nothing ran against the
account's own Gitea. Raw logs stayed in the session scratchpad.

| Fact                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | Evidence                                                                                |
