| ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **`REVERSE_PROXY_TRUSTED_PROXIES` does not restrict who may send `X-WEBAUTH-USER`.** With the trusted range set to `10.99.0.0/16` and to `192.0.2.1`, a header sent from `127.0.0.1` still logged in as a user and as the site admin (`/-/admin` 200). The setting only feeds `X-Forwarded-For` real-IP parsing. The header also authenticates git over HTTP, and a login it creates persists as a cookie session.                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | `services/auth/reverseproxy.go:37-38`; `routers/common/middleware.go:33-34`; access log |
| **With `ENABLE_REVERSE_PROXY_AUTHENTICATION_API=true` a header-asserted admin mints tokens for any user** (`POST /users/{u}/tokens` → 201); a non-admin header gets 403, and `Sudo:` works only for admins. Minting for another user needs `write:user`, and a token cannot mint a broader scope than its own.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | `routers/api/v1/api.go:396,1080-1084`; `user/app.go:129-144`                            |
| **Org teams confine a `write:repository` token; a site admin's token reaches every repo.** A writer of `grp-a` cloned and pushed `grp-a/app` and got 404 on `grp-b/app`; the site-admin `mate` model pushed to a repo it was never a member of. `restricted: true` also hides public repos; per-user `max_repo_creation: 0` and `allow_create_organization: false` close the remaining doors.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | three token identities; `repo_permission.go:440-458`                                    |
| **Protected tags have no admin override.** With `v*` protected to team `releasers`, git push, `POST …/tags` and `POST …/releases` with a new tag were refused for writers, repo admins, org owners and the site admin; a releaser succeeded; move and delete were refused for all others. Publishing a draft release onto a protected tag answers **500**.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | `hook_pre_receive.go:396-431`; `protected_tag.go:126-149`                               |
| **Protected file patterns are absolute on direct push and per protected branch.** `.gitea/workflows/**` on `main` refused a direct push from every identity, the site admin included; a catch-all `**` rule extends it to every branch. On the merge path `block_admin_merge_override=true` stops admins, and `bypass_allowlist_teams` is the one exception.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | `hook_pre_receive.go:251-330`; `services/pull/check.go:194-208`                         |
| **Gitea Actions secrets reach any writer.** A writer's push of a new branch with its own workflow, and a same-repo pull request, both received the repo secret and the org secret (hashes matched). Only fork PRs withhold secrets. There is no environment, required-reviewer or ref gate for secrets, and no OIDC id-token (`ACTIONS_ID_TOKEN_REQUEST_URL` absent; `permissions: id-token` parsed and ignored).                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | job logs; `models/secret/secret.go:162-167`; `permission_parser.go:120-124`             |
| **The automatic job token is a bot (id `-2`) that writes by default.** It pushed a new branch, was refused a protected tag and protected `main`, and a job-level `permissions: {contents: read}` clamped it. The repo/owner `token_permission_mode` has no REST API.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | job log; `repo_unit_actions.go:15-82`                                                   |
| **Webhooks identify the pusher and are signed.** `push` carries `pusher` and `sender`, `create` only `sender`; `X-Gitea-Signature` is hex HMAC-SHA256 of the raw body (also `X-Hub-Signature-256`).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | four deliveries, all signatures verified                                                |
| **Template repositories expand `${REPO_NAME}`, `${REPO_OWNER}`, `${REPO_DESCRIPTION}`** via `.gitea/template` on `POST …/generate`; migrating `github.com/zerops-recipe-apps/go-hello-world-app` works; `mirror: true` makes a repo read-only; the push-mirror API exists.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | `generate` and `migrate` calls                                                          |
| **Bots are first-class but CLI-only.** `admin user create --user-type bot` makes a password-less, token-only user that pushes like any writer; the REST API cannot set the type. `must_change_password` blocks token and git auth too. Admins can list and delete another user's tokens by id or name.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | CLI + API calls; `models/user/user.go:49-67`                                            |
| **An OIDC login source maps a `groups` claim onto org teams at every login.** `--group-team-map` with `--group-team-map-removal`, `--admin-group` and `--restricted-group`: first login auto-registered and joined the mapped teams; a changed claim moved the user between teams and toggled site admin and restricted. An existing web session is **not** re-validated, and the source cannot authenticate git or the API.                                                                                                                                                                                                        | `routers/web/auth/oauth.go:233-267,402-403`; `source_sync.go:18-90`                     |
| **The Zerops-backed prototype mirrors live roles into teams.** Six Zerops identities logged into Gitea: `READ_ONLY` on stage → `readers`; `ADMIN` on the Mate's dev project → `readers writers`; `BASIC_USER` on stage and production → `readers releasers`; the org owner → site admin and all teams. Removing production from one identity's grants moved it to `readers` at its next login; deleting another identity made the provider refuse (`user/info 401`) while its Gitea account kept its team until something removes it. One org `READ_ONLY` token computed the same rights for all 25 members without any user token. | prototype log; team member reads                                                        |
| **Gitea hands every Actions job all org and repo secrets; only fork pull requests are excluded.** `GetSecretsOfTask` loads the owner's and the repository's secrets for any task, skips them only for a fork pull request not triggered by `pull_request_target`, and narrows them only through a reusable workflow's `secrets:` mapping. There is no environment or protected-ref scoping. Source read, not measured.                                                                                                                                                                                                              | `models/secret/secret.go:160-194` on `main` (release v1.27.3)                           |
| **Gitea's API covers what Mate would drive.** Actions runs, jobs, logs, rerun, cancel and workflow dispatch; secrets and variables at org and repository scope; runners and registration tokens at repository, org and admin scope; repository archives; commit statuses; branch and tag protections; webhooks; pull-request merge. Source read.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | `templates/swagger/v1-swagger.generated.json` on `main`                                 |
| **Gitea is an OAuth2 provider.** Authorization code with PKCE, confidential and public clients, granular scopes since v1.23. The docs say nothing about CORS on the token endpoint, which a browser client needs. Docs read.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | `docs.gitea.com/development/oauth2-provider`                                            |

## A member's throwaway token as their identity — 2026-09-15

Measured as a `NO_ACCESS` member with _can create projects_ in the test org `Mate`
(`m1VrZPJlSnAmnYAvfuEZEg`), on resources that member created and deleted after: one LIGHT project
tagged `probe:q21-2026-09-15`, a personal token, a Mate-shaped key and eight throwaway tokens. Five
timed rounds from the probe host. The member's token list, personal tokens and projects read the same
before and after. Answers Q-17 and Q-21.

| Fact                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | Evidence                                                           |
| --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| **A member below `ADMIN` mints a grant-less token that names them.** The member minted a `NO_ACCESS` integration token with no project grants, from a personal token and from a plain sign-in session alike (no sudo step). It authenticated on its first call; `/user/info` answered with the token id; its self-read returned its name, `created`, `createdByUser` = the member's user id, `NO_ACCESS` and no grants. A Mate-shaped key (`NO_ACCESS` + `BASIC_USER` on the member's project) found the member in `GET /client/{id}/user/list` (`ACTIVE`, `NO_ACCESS`) and their `OWNER` role in the project's `userRoles`, and could read the throwaway's detail by id. So a Mate can learn who is at its door, and their role there, without ever holding that person's token. | five rounds of mint → self-read → member list → project → `DELETE` |
| **A throwaway reaches metadata and nothing else.** With the grant-less token: minting a token, grant-less or with a grant on the member's project → `403 notAllowedForIntegrationTokenWithoutDelegation`; raising its own grants or deleting itself → `notAllowedForIntegrationToken`; creating a personal token → `notAllowedForApplicationToken`; reading the project, listing projects, creating one → `insufficientPermissions`. It read the org's member list (12, with emails and roles) and the metadata of the creator's other tokens (no values). A captured throwaway therefore cannot be turned into a pass for another Mate, and only the person's own credential can delete it.                                                                                      | each call with the throwaway before its deletion                   |
| **The round trip is a few seconds.** Each call took 0.6–0.7 s from the probe host: mint ≈ 650 ms; the door's four reads (`/user/info`, self-read, member list, project) ≈ 2.6 s one after another; after `DELETE` returned, the token answered `401` within ≈ 0.6 s.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | medians of five rounds                                             |
| **A member below `ADMIN` with _can create projects_ owns the project they create.** The member's `POST /client/{id}/project` answered `200` (a token of that shape gets `400 userNotFound` there, row in the auth section above); the project's `userRoles` listed the member as `OWNER`; they wrote project env and tags (`OWNER`/`ADMIN` only) and deleted the project. The OpenAPI says the same for every role below `ADMIN`. `READ_ONLY` with the flag was not tried.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | project read and writes as the member                              |

## A member's rights and the tokens they made — 2026-09-15

Measured in the test org `Mate` (`m1VrZPJlSnAmnYAvfuEZEg`) as its owner and a member (normally
`NO_ACCESS` + _can create projects_), on two LIGHT projects
tagged `probe:q22-2026-09-15` and five probe tokens, all deleted after. The member's role, flags and
custom project roles were restored; the org read back at 10 members, 8 tokens and 8 projects.
Answers Q-22 and most of Q-16.

| Fact                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | Evidence                                                                  |
| ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **A member can be `READ_ONLY` with _can create projects_, and then owns what they create.** `PUT /client-user/{id}` with that pair answered `200` and read back unchanged. The member listed all 9 of the org's projects and read one they did not create, where an env write was `403`. Their `POST /client/{id}/project` made them its `OWNER` — stored as a custom project role on them — and they wrote its env and tags. They minted a grant-less throwaway that named them, and a Mate-shaped key on their project resolved them as `READ_ONLY` in the org and `OWNER` there. | the member's calls, the owner's reads of `/client-user/{id}` and `/roles` |
| **Lowering a person does not lower the tokens they made.** The member minted a token with `BASIC_USER` on their project and it wrote env there. The owner then set the member's custom role there to `READ_ONLY` (`PUT /client-user/{id}/roles`): the member's own env write became `403` and minting `BASIC_USER` there answered `roleLevelExceeded`, but the earlier token kept its `BASIC_USER` grant and its env write still answered `200`.                                                                                                                                    | the same write before and after the lowering                              |
| **Regenerating a token hands it to whoever regenerates it.** The owner's `PUT /client/{id}/integration-token/{tokenId}/regenerate` on the member's Mate-shaped key made the owner its `createdByUser`; the old value answered `401` at once and the new value worked.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | token detail before and after                                             |
| **A member who holds tokens cannot be removed.** The owner's `DELETE /client-user/{id}` without `force`, while the member held a token they had created, answered `409 deleteExistingApplicationTokens` ("No application tokens created by this user must exist before proceeding. Please delete them first.") and the member stayed `ACTIVE`. The OpenAPI says `force=true` deletes those tokens too; not tried, because putting a member back takes their acceptance from an e-mail (`PUT /user/authorize-client` needs that e-mail's token).                                     | the refusal, then the member list                                         |

## The broker's key and a Gitea-layout archive — 2026-09-15

Measured in the test org `Mate` as its owner: two LIGHT projects tagged
`probe:q20q18-2026-09-15`, one `nodejs@22` service and one probe token, all deleted after; the org
read back at 8 tokens and 8 projects, as before. Answers Q-18 and Q-20.

| Fact                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | Evidence                                            |
| --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------- |
| **An org `READ_ONLY` token raised to `BASIC_USER` on one project writes there and nowhere else, and takes new grants in place.** `/user/info` showed `READ_ONLY`; it listed the org's projects, wrote env on the granted project and got `403` on the other. After the owner added a `BASIC_USER` grant on the other project with `PUT /client/{id}/integration-token/{tokenId}`, the unchanged value wrote there on the first call (0.7 s).                                                              | the same writes before and after the grant          |
| **That token deploys an archive on its own.** `POST /service-stack/{id}/app-version`, `PUT /app-version/{id}/upload` (the archive as `application/octet-stream`) and `PUT /app-version/{id}/build-and-deploy` with the `zerops.yaml` text and its setup, all as that token: the process `FINISHED` and the app version went `ACTIVE` in 59 s.                                                                                                                                                             | a two-file app whose build runs `test -f server.js` |
| **The platform keeps an archive's top-level folder, so Gitea's default archive does not build.** Gitea serves `git archive` output prefixed with the repository name unless `[repository] PREFIX_ARCHIVE_FILES = false` (default `true`, `modules/setting/repository.go:184`, v1.27.3). Reproduced with `git archive`: the prefixed archive went `BUILD_FAILED` in 30 s, because the build's `test -f server.js` found nothing at the root; the same commit without the prefix is the `ACTIVE` row above. | both layouts from one commit, one after the other   |

## What a container sees of its project-mates — 2026-09-16

Measured in Onboarding: one LIGHT project tagged `probe:envscope-2026-09-16` holding `alpha`
(`nodejs@22`, carrying one plain and one sensitive variable whose values were invented sentinels),
`db` (`postgresql@16`) and `runner` (`nodejs@22`), deployed with a server that reported back the
names of everything it could see; the project was deleted after and the org read back at 9 tokens and
10 projects, as before. Settles where the Actions runners live.

| Fact                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | Evidence                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **A container reads only its own service's variables.** `runner`'s `process.env` carried 151 keys and its live env store `/etc/zerops-zembed/env.json` 123 — not one `alpha_*` or `db_*` among them, and neither sentinel value appeared in either, the sensitive one included. What is there is the service's own (`hostname`, `serviceId`, `projectId`, `appVersionId`, `zeropsSubdomain`, `PATH`) and the platform's `ZEROPS_*`. The store is `0644` and the app runs as `zerops` (uid 2023), so that is what job code sees.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | the deployed probe, serving its own key names over its subdomain                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| **The gate is the project's `envIsolation`, and its default is `service`.** `none` lets every service in the project read them, `service` only the service itself, `service@name` adds one more, `-service@name` excludes one; under `none` a sibling's fully resolved values land as `{hostname}_{key}`, secrets included. Explicit `${host_var}` references work in every mode and resolve at container start.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | the platform's import schema, `../zcp/internal/schema/testdata/import_yml_schema.json`; zcp's `docs/spec-zerops-env-lifecycle.md:95-117`, measured 2026-05-28 (`core_JWT_SECRET` and `zcp_ZCP_API_KEY` visible under `none`; zero `db_*` under `service`) |
| **Project-level variables ignore isolation** — they reach every service and every build container. `ZCP_API_KEY` is one of a Mate project's project variables, so every container in a Mate's project can read the Mate's key, whatever the isolation.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | zcp's `docs/spec-zerops-env-lifecycle.md:99`; `z3-eval`'s project envs in `packages/client-runtime/src/zerops/operations/__fixtures__/add-mariadb.json:1268`                                                                                              |
| **A Gitea project gets that default, and its runners live in it.** `giteaRecipe.ts` sets no isolation anywhere, and `buildGiteaRunnerImportYaml` imports the runner addon into the Gitea project, with only its own `RUNNER_REGISTRATION_TOKEN` in its vault.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | `packages/client-runtime/src/zerops/giteaRecipe.ts:26-53`                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| **A sensitive variable is `REDACTED` to `READ_ONLY` and in clear to `BASIC_USER` and above on that project.** With a sentinel written as a sensitive service variable: the owner, and a `NO_ACCESS` token holding `BASIC_USER` on the project, read it in clear from `GET /service-stack/{id}/user-data` and `…/env`; an org `READ_ONLY` token with no grants, one with `BASIC_USER` elsewhere, and a `NO_ACCESS` token with `READ_ONLY` on the project all got `sensitive: true, content: "REDACTED"` from both. The stack `GET` carries no variable values at all.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | probe `probe:readonlyenv-2026-09-16`, one project, four tokens, all deleted; counts back to 9 tokens / 10 projects                                                                                                                                        |
| **Every live Mate project runs `envIsolation: none`, and so do three stage and production projects made the old way.** `POST /project/search` (the call zcp uses) shows `envIsolation: none` and `sshIsolation: vpn service@zcp` on `zerops-mate10`, `scratch-playground`, `acme-docs-dev` and `beviro-crm-dev` (each with a zcp service) and on `beviro-crm-prod`, `beviro-crm-stage` and `Acme Docs - stage` (no zcp service today), each carrying `ZCP_API_KEY` as a _project_ variable; `mate-gitea`, `hello-go - production` and `Acme Docs - production` run `service` with no key. The org's token list pairs each key-carrying project with a `zcp-<project>` token, `NO_ACCESS` + `ADMIN` on that one project — so a production project holds a key with `ADMIN` on itself in every one of its containers. | read-only `POST /project/search` per project and `GET /client/{id}/integration-token/list`, Onboarding                                                                                                                                                    |

## A job's token, the queued-job webhook and the browser's exchange — 2026-09-16

Measured on the local lab, Gitea 1.27.2 (source at the same tag) with act_runner 3.5.0, in
`probe-` orgs and repos; Gitea and the runners stopped after, `app.ini` restored. Answers Q-19 and
Q-25, and the Gitea half of Q-27.

| Fact                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | Evidence                                                        |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **A job's token is one identity for every spelling, and it dies with the job.** `secrets.GITEA_TOKEN`, `secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN` and `github.token` are the same 40-character value (the raw env vars `GITEA_TOKEN`/`GITHUB_TOKEN` are unset). `GET /api/v1/user` answers `id: -2`, `login: gitea-actions`, `login_name: @gitea-actions/{taskId}` — the _task_ id, one per job, not the run (`models/user/user_system.go:61`, `services/auth/oauth2.go:135`). Polled every 100 ms, the token answered `401` 0.11 s after the job's `completed_at`; a second job in the same run reading the first job's token got `401`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | six jobs in `probe-q25/a`; the poller's log                     |
| **The token proves its repository in one call, and `GET /repos/{claimed}` alone does not.** With the job's token, `GET /repos/{owner}/{repo}/actions/jobs/{taskId}` answered `200` for the job's own repository and `404` for every other — same org, other org, and public ones — and `404` for other tasks' ids under the own repository. The body carries `run_id`, `head_sha`, `head_branch`, `name`, `status`, `runner_id`. `GET /repos/{claimed}` is `200` for any _public_ repository on the instance, so it identifies nothing. Under `permissions: {contents: read}` the proving call answers `403` — a broker needs the workflow's default permissions or an explicit `actions: read`.                                                                                                                                                                                           | the call table in `gitea-lab/probe/q25-joblog*.txt`             |
| **What else a job's token reaches.** Its own repository in full (clone, push a branch, statuses, issues, runs, jobs); `404` on sibling and foreign repositories, on cloning them, on creating repositories; `403` on runners, secrets, variables and admin routes. But `GET /user/repos`, `GET /user/orgs` and `GET /user/actions/runners` list **every** repository, org and runner on the instance by name (access to their contents is refused), and it can open issues on any public repository.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | the same table                                                  |
| **A queued job fires `workflow_job` with no runner online, and a late runner takes it at once.** With zero runners registered, a push produced `workflow_job` `action: queued` and `workflow_run` `action: requested` on both an org-level and a repository-level hook 3.5 s after the push, carrying `workflow_job.labels`, `repository.full_name`, `run_id` and `head_sha`, no token anywhere in the payload. A runner registered at org scope 101 s later (registration token by `POST /orgs/{org}/actions/runners/registration-token` — `GET` is a `404`) took the job 0.83 s after its daemon started; `in_progress` and `completed` deliveries followed on both hooks. While it waited, `GET …/actions/runs` and `/runs/{id}/jobs` showed it `queued`; `GET …/actions/tasks` showed nothing.                                                                                         | `gitea-lab/probe/q27-gitea-webhooks.ndjson`                     |
| **A job nobody takes is cancelled, not failed, after `ABANDONED_JOB_TIMEOUT` (default 24 h) at the next 6-hourly sweep** (`modules/setting/actions.go:132`, `services/cron/tasks_actions.go:45`, run at start). With the timeout set to 30 s and a restart to fire the sweep: `completed`/`cancelled` webhooks 62 s after the push. There is no delivery-history API in 1.27.2 (`…/hooks/{id}/deliveries` is `404`; the `hook_task` table showed all 20 deliveries `204`). A runner's `status` in the API stayed `online` after its process was killed.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | the same log; the `hook_task` rows                              |
| **A browser on another origin completes the PKCE exchange once `[cors]` allows its origin.** `[cors] ENABLED=true`, `ALLOW_DOMAIN=http://localhost:8099`, `HEADERS=Content-Type,Authorization`: the preflight and the `POST` to `/login/oauth/access_token` carried `Access-Control-Allow-Origin` (`routers/web/web.go:616` wraps `access_token`, `userinfo`, `keys` and `introspect` in the CORS handler; `/authorize` is a top-level navigation and needs none). A Chromium page on that origin ran the whole flow — verifier and S256 challenge, _Authorize Application_, the exchange with no client secret, `GET /api/v1/user` → `200` as the signed-in user. The same page served from `http://127.0.0.1:8099` got `TypeError: Failed to fetch` on both calls: `ALLOW_DOMAIN` matches the origin string literally, so every origin the app runs from, port included, must be listed. | `gitea-lab/probe/q19-headers.log`, the page's `window.__RESULT` |

## Isolation flipped live, the key moved, the tag budget, a service asleep — 2026-09-16

Measured in Onboarding with the owner's token: four LIGHT projects tagged `probe:{envflip,keymove,tags,wake}-2026-09-16`, one Mate-shaped by the platform's development-container recipe, all deleted; the org read back at 10 projects, 9 tokens and 11 members, as before. Answers Q-23, Q-24, Q-26 and Q-27.

| Fact                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | Evidence                                             |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ---------------------------------------------------- |
| **A project is born with `envIsolation: service`, and a write to it reaches running containers in seconds without a restart — for the store, not for a running process.** Project variables read through `POST /project/search` (`items[0].envList`, entries with `id`), update with `PUT /project-env/{id}` `{key, content, sensitive}`, create with `POST /project/{pid}/env`, delete with `DELETE /project-env/{id}`. Under `none`, a sibling `alpha`'s 29 variables — its `sensitive: true` sentinel among them, verbatim — sat in the probe container's `/etc/zerops-zembed/env.json` and in its process's `process.env`. Seven seconds after the `PUT` to `service` (the first poll; the call itself took ~5 s), the store held 123 keys and no `alpha_*`, same pid, uptime still rising; `process.env` kept all 29 until `PUT /service-stack/{id}/restart`, after which both were clean. Flipped back to `none`, the 29 were back in the store within 7 s. Project-level variables stay visible under `service`; isolation governs sibling _service_ variables only.                                                                            | `probe_q23.py`, `q23-results.json`                   |
| **zcp runs with `ZCP_API_KEY` as a service variable, and the platform leaves the project-level entry deleted.** `PUT /project/{pid}/first-class-recipe/development-container` `{serviceImportYaml, recipeSource: "zeropsio/zcp", createIntegrationToken: true}` created the `zcp-<project>` token (`NO_ACCESS` + `ADMIN` on the project), a pseudo-member `token-…@zerops.io`, an unconsumed delegation `NO_ACCESS` + `canCreateProjects`, and a project with `envIsolation: none`, `sshIsolation: vpn service@zcp` and **`ZCP_API_KEY` as a project variable, `sensitive: false`**, readable in clear. After writing the value as a sensitive service variable on `zcp`, deleting the project entry and restarting: `GET /service-stack/{zcp}/env` listed `ZCP_API_KEY` `type: USER`, `sensitive: true`; the application log (`GET /project/{pid}/log` → signed `url` + `facility=16&serviceStackId=`) showed an unchanged boot with no auth error across two restarts; the project entry had not reappeared after five minutes and a second restart. Deleting the token removed the pseudo-member with it (12 → 11) while the project still existed. | `probe_q24.py`, `q24-results.json`, `q24-extra.json` |
| **`POST /project/search` is `401 notAuthorized` to every integration token whose org role is `NO_ACCESS`** — `ADMIN` on the project (a zcp key's own shape), `BASIC_USER` and `READ_ONLY` alike. An owner reading a _sensitive_ project variable gets a 442-character hex blob whose head decodes to `age-encryption.org/v`, not the value and not `REDACTED`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | the same probe                                       |
| **Tags have one byte budget and no count limit.** `PUT /project/{id}` `{name, description, tagList, publicIpV4Shared, maxCreditLimit}` (omit `userRoles`, an object on the wire) accepts any `tagList` whose compact JSON is ≤ 65 534 bytes and refuses 65 535 with `400 invalidUserInput` — 2 000 tags of 29 characters pass at exactly 65 534, and so do 100 tags of 652; 2 509 tags of the registry's shape fit, 2 510 do not; a single tag of 65 506 characters passes. Duplicates, empty strings, spaces and non-ASCII are stored verbatim. `GET /project/{id}` grows from 644 B to 66 KB at the ceiling while its latency stays ~650 ms; `GET /client/{id}/project?limit=200` carried 60 KB with 2 000 tags on one project. One observation: `DELETE /project/{id}` answered `400 unableToCreateProcess` on a project holding a 65 506-character tag, and succeeded once the tag list was shrunk.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | `probe_q26*.py`, `q26*-results.json`                 |
| **`BASIC_USER` on a project stops and starts a service; each direction settles in ~6 s.** `PUT /service-stack/{id}/stop` → `ACTIVE → STOPPING → STOPPED` in 6.0–6.2 s, `…/start` → `STOPPED → STARTING → ACTIVE` in 6.0–6.1 s (three cycles; a `startWithoutCode` service with no application — a real one adds its own start and healthcheck). `READ_ONLY` on the project: both `403 insufficientPermissions`, the stack read `200`. Stopped, the stack's `startOnProjectStart` flips to `false`, `verticalAutoscaling` reads `null`, and `GET /service-stack/{id}/container` still lists the container with identical `currentHardwareResource` — nothing in the API says whether a stopped service is charged.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | `probe_q27*.py`, `q27*-results.json`                 |

## A `BASIC_USER` project token does everything zcp does; a hostname is `[a-z][a-z0-9]*` — 2026-09-16

Guide 0.1's gate: the four operations not yet measured at project `BASIC_USER`, plus the hostname rule the runner services need. Onboarding org, a throwaway project (`probe:basic-user-2026-09-16`), a token `NO_ACCESS` at the org with one `BASIC_USER` grant; deleted afterwards, counts equal before and after (9 tokens, 10 projects). Scripts `probe_q01_basic_user.py`, `probe_q01b_envupdate_hostname.py`, `probe_q01c_logs.py`.

| Claim                                                                                        | Measured                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `BASIC_USER` enables the subdomain of a service it deployed                                  | `PUT /service-stack/{id}/enable-subdomain-access` → `200` on the first try after the deploy process reached `FINISHED`; the subdomain served the deployed file anonymously (`200`, 58 B). The whole deploy path before it (`import`, `app-version`, `upload`, `build-and-deploy`, `GET /process/{id}`) also answered `200` at that role.                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| `BASIC_USER` has full CRUD on the project's variables                                        | `POST /project/{pid}/env` `200`; `POST /project/search` `200` with the entry in `envList` in clear; `PUT /project-env/{id}` `200`; `DELETE /project-env/{id}` `200`, the entry gone. Only `PUT /project/{pid}` stays closed: `403 insufficientPermissions` with the project's own `name, description, tagList, publicIpV4Shared, maxCreditLimit` (the tag measurement of 2026-09-15 confirmed).                                                                                                                                                                   |
| The update body needs the key, and the create answers with a process id                      | `PUT /project-env/{id}` with `{content}` alone is `400 invalidUserInput` `{"key":["field is required"]}` — for the owner too; `{key, content}` is `200` and reads back as written. `POST /project/{pid}/env` returns a process id, not the entry's id: the entry's id comes only from `POST /project/search` → `items[0].envList[].id`, so an update or delete built off the create response answers `404`.                                                                                                                                                       |
| `BASIC_USER` reads the project's logs                                                        | `GET /project/{pid}/log` → `200` `{accessToken, expiration (+1 h), url, urlInfo, urlPlain, urlUi}`; the signed `url` unfiltered → `200`, 136 rows. `serviceStackId=<id>` alone filters to one service (11 rows, facilities 3 and 9); `facility=16` is the builder (91 rows, `tag: zbuilder@…`) whose rows carry no service id, so `facility=16&serviceStackId=…` is 0 rows by construction; `facility=0` behaves as unset. Rows carry no `serviceStackId` field — it is a server-side filter only. `GET /service-stack/{id}/log` does not exist (`404 notFound`). |
| A service hostname is lowercase letters and digits, not starting with a digit, no `-` or `_` | `POST /project/{id}/service-stack/import` (owner): `runner-acme`, `runner_acme`, `runnerAcme`, `9runner` → `400 serviceStackNameInvalid` "Service stack name is invalid.", `metadata: null`; `runneracme`, `runner9acme` → `200`. So a group's runner is `runner{slug without dashes}`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| The documented 25-character hostname limit is not what the API enforces                      | `GET /settings/import-project-yml-json-schema.json` promises "maximum 25 characters"; a 26-character hostname was accepted, and a bisect put the ceiling at 40 accepted, 41 refused (`serviceStackNameInvalid`). Generated names keep to the promised 25.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |

## An app version's name is write-only; a tag's webhook sha depends on who made the tag — 2026-09-16

Onboarding org, a throwaway static service (`probe:appversion-2026-09-16`, `probe_q02_appversion.py`, `probe_q02_userdata.py`), two deploys named on creation; the lab Gitea 1.27.2 for the webhook (`probe_q02_gitea.sh`). Counts equal before and after (9 tokens, 10 projects).

| Claim                                                                                                                                  | Measured                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| The app-version API never returns `name`                                                                                               | `POST /service-stack/{id}/app-version {name}` answers `build, clientId, created, id, lastUpdate, projectId, sequence, serviceStackId, status, uploadUrl, userDataList` — no `name`. `GET /service-stack/{id}/app-version` (envelope `{count, limit, list, offset, total}`) and `GET /app-version/{id}` carry 16 keys and no `name`; the sent string is nowhere in the raw response. `POST /service-stack/{id}/app-version/search` is `404 notFound`. Activeness is `status`: the newest is `ACTIVE`, the previous flips to `BACKUP`; `GET /service-stack/{id}.activeAppVersion` is the same object, also without `name`. |
| The name survives as the service's `appVersionName` variable, for the active version only                                              | `GET /service-stack/{id}.userData[]` holds `{key: "appVersionName", content: "<the name, byte-exact, spaces included>"}` beside `appVersionId`, `hostname`, `serviceId`, `projectId`, `zeropsSubdomain`. A deployed commit named by its sha is therefore read back from the service's env, never from the version list.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| `BASIC_USER` on a project imports and deletes a service                                                                                | `POST /project/{id}/service-stack/import` with `startWithoutCode: true` → `200`, `CREATING → ACTIVE`; `DELETE /service-stack/{id}` → `200` with a process body (`id` = the process; `GET /process/{id}` `RUNNING → FINISHED`); afterwards `GET /service-stack/{id}` is `400 serviceStackNotFound`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| Gitea's `create` webhook for an annotated tag names the commit when the tag came through the API and the tag object when git pushed it | `POST /repos/{o}/{r}/tags {tag_name, target, message}`: `create.sha` = the commit's sha, `push.after` the same; `git tag -a` + push: `create.sha` = the tag object's sha (`git/refs/tags/{tag}` → `object.sha`), `push.after` the same, `push.head_commit.id` the commit. Both refs peel to the commit via `GET /repos/{o}/{r}/git/tags/{sha}` → `object: {type: commit, sha}`. `create` payload keys: `ref, ref_type, repository, sender, sha`; the API path fires `push` as well as `create`; `pusher.login` and `sender` are set on both.                                                                             |

## The backbone's first live run: Gitea, the broker, a sign-in, a Mate's credential, a stage deployed — 2026-09-16

Onboarding org, three throwaway projects tagged `probe:e2e-2026-09-16` (the Gitea project imported from `zeropsio/gitea-mate`, a Mate-shaped project, a stage), all deleted; counts equal before and after (9 tokens, 10 projects). Scripts `probe_e2e_*.py`. Every step of guide 1.2–1.6, 3.6, 5.3, 5.4 passed once five broker fixes landed (`gitea-mate` `8c454cb`–`fa06366`).

| Claim                                                                                         | Measured                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| An imported variable may not start with `ZEROPS_`                                             | `POST /project/{id}/service-stack/import` (owner) → `400 userDataZeropsPrefixForbidden` "Custom environment variables with 'ZEROPS_' prefix are forbidden" for `ZEROPS_FOO` and `zerops_foo` (case-insensitive); `ZEROPSFOO`, `ZEROPS`, `MATE_ZEROPS_TOKEN` → `200`. The platform uses the prefix itself (`ZEROPS_appVersionId`, `ZEROPS_appVersionName` on every container). The broker's four Zerops variables are `MATE_ZEROPS_*`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| A service's ports carry routing flags in the search, not in the direct read                   | `GET /service-stack/{id}` ports are `{protocol, port, description, scheme}` — no `httpRouting`/`portRouting`; `POST /service-stack/search` carries both. Same service, two shapes; a reader of the direct shape decides HTTP by `scheme`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| The owner reads a service's sensitive variables in clear                                      | `GET /service-stack/{id}/user-data` with the org owner's own token returns `GITEA_ADMIN_TOKEN` and `GITEA_ADMIN_PASSWORD` in clear (list under `list`); the `BASIC_USER`-and-above rule of the same day holds for the owner too.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| The whole Gitea project is up in under three minutes                                          | project 4.4 s, broker token 0.8 s, import 1.4 s; `db`, `volume`, `web`, `broker` all `ACTIVE` 167 s after the import; `web` restarts itself five times while `init.sh`'s secrets propagate. Gitea's `admin auth add-oauth` run while the broker's discovery URL still answered 502 wrote the login source without creating the provider (no sign-in link until a Gitea restart) — the recipe now waits for the broker before adding it.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| The rights loop builds a group in 80 s, and a sign-in lands in the right teams within seconds | registry tags written → org `e2e`, teams `read`/`write`/`release` (units read / write / write), `e2e/group` private with `main` protected (merge whitelist `release`) and `env/*` (push whitelist the admin), one org webhook, the Mate's bot restricted in `read`: 80 s. OIDC: `GET /user/oauth2/zerops` → broker `/oidc/authorize` → the consent URL's `rid` in 1.4 s; `POST /oidc/complete` by throwaway 134 ms; the callback lands a Gitea session; user `u-{id}` is `is_admin` (org OWNER) and in all three teams after the pass the token issuance triggers. Gitea 1.27.2: `/api/v1/user` is `403` to a session cookie; pages carry no CSRF token (Origin-checked); `/api/v1/admin/auth/sources` does not exist. |
| A Mate gets its credential and repository from the broker                                     | `POST /mate/credential` by throwaway → `200` in 0.98 s, `minted: true`, generation 1; `rotate` → generations 2 and 3. `POST /mate/repository` as the bot → `e2e/api` in 2.2 s; the bot pushed `mate/{bot}` and opened PR #1; the owner (in `write`) merged it.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| A merge deploys a stage through the webhook in 15 s and serves in under three minutes         | a merge into `e2e/api` `main` → app version named by the merge sha +15 s, `mate/deploy/stage/api` `pending` → `success` (description the version id), the subdomain serving the new file +173 s. A queued deploy must run on the broker's own context: on the webhook's it was `context canceled` before its first read. Catch-up: broker stopped, a commit merged, broker started → healthz in 7 s, exactly one deploy of the newer sha +9 s. With the broker down nobody signs in to Gitea.                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| A group's runner appears on its first job and the action deploys with a job token             | `workflow_job` `queued` → `runnere2e` imported, `ACTIVE` +117 s, registered at org scope, took the job in host mode; `zeropsio/gitea-mate/actions/deploy@v1` → `POST /deploy` `202` and `GET /deploy/{id}` → `active` for a `gitea-actions` token, the job green in 71 s. A `uses:` ref must exist as a tag on GitHub (`@v1` failed "reference not found" until the tag was pushed).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |

## The first Mate on an emptied org, through the released client: the door's member list — 2026-09-16

Test org `Mate`, wiped to nothing, then one project made from the localhost client (fork `7ea884702`, which stands Gitea up inside _New project_) against the released server 0.11.0. Gitea's four services and the Mate's `zcp` were `ACTIVE` in about three minutes; the door then refused every connect.

| Claim                                                                                            | Measured                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `GET /client/{id}/user/list` answers `{ clientUserList: [...] }` — not `items`, not a bare array | Read as the org owner and as a Mate-shaped token (`NO_ACCESS` + `BASIC_USER` on the one project, minted and deleted as `probe:door-shape-2026-09-16`): both `200`, both `clientUserList` of rows `id, userId, roleCode, status, user, clientId, canCreateProjects, canEditFinances, canViewFinances`. The 0.11.0 server's door read `items` and answered `500 zerops_membership_check_failed` ("This org's member list was not in the expected shape.") on every attempt — the container log, five times. The client rendered it as "The environment could not authorize the connection." Fixed for 0.11.1. |
| The Mate's key is not among its service variables, yet its server read its own project           | `GET /service-stack/{zcp}/user-data` listed no `ZCP_API_KEY`; the project's `envList` was empty (the isolate step deleted it); the door still passed its step-0 project read. Answered the same evening: zcp's own env store (the zembed document `ZeropsAgentAuth` reads its flags from) carries `ZCP_API_KEY`, captured from the project env at first boot before the move; the Mate server reads it from there.                                                                                                                                                                                          |

## D20 driven end to end: the broker booted before Gitea published its admin token — 2026-09-17

Test org `Mate`, emptied, then `probe_d20_run.py` (tag `probe:d20-2026-09-17`): Gitea imported (gitea-mate `e48cc97`, the loop that delivers a Mate's access), the group and the Mate registered, the Mate made with the client's `zcp@1` YAML, the broker's token widened to the Mate project; then nothing but watching.

| Claim                                                                                                                              | Measured                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Order on a fresh account: the Mate's container is up long before Gitea                                                             | Mate `zcp` ACTIVE 40 s after registration; Gitea `web` ACTIVE at +154 s, `broker` at +174 s (broker process listening 07:14:06Z).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| The broker can boot before `web` has published `GITEA_ADMIN_TOKEN`, and then holds the reference unresolved for life               | Its first pass at 07:14:06Z could not reach `web:3000` (connection refused: Gitea not listening yet); every pass from 07:17:06Z on, three minutes apart for twenty minutes, answered `gitea: 401 … invalid username, password or token` on `GET /admin/users` — the same on the OAuth2 client registration and the deploy pass. The value `${web_GITEA_ADMIN_TOKEN}` was captured by the process at start, before Gitea's `admin-init.sh` had written it, and the broker reads its env only at start. The Mate never got its variables (20 min). The 2026-09-16 runs worked on timing alone. The fix in flight: the broker treats an empty or `${…}` value as not arrived and fetches both admin credentials from `web`'s user-data through the Zerops API, and re-fetches on a 401.                                                                                                                              |
| Once the broker holds the token, one pass delivers a registered Mate's access                                                      | `PUT /service-stack/{broker}/restart` at 07:33Z; the broker listened at 07:33:48Z, its first pass planned 13 and applied 13 (`problems: 0`), and 20 s after the restart the Mate's `zcp` service carried `GITEA_URL` (Gitea's public origin), `MATE_BROKER_URL` (the broker's) and `GITEA_TOKEN` (sensitive) — nobody asked, nothing restarted. Gitea's side of that pass, read with a temporary integration token: the bot `mate-{projectId}` exists, `restricted`, in the org's `read` team (`write` and `release` empty, `Owners` = `admin`), the group repo `imperial-titan/group` and one org hook exist; the delivered `GITEA_TOKEN` authenticates as the bot (`GET /api/v1/user`), and `POST /mate/repository {name: hello}` with it answered `200 {fullName: imperial-titan/hello, created: true}`. The bot's full name came out as the project's name (`Imperial Titan - dev`), not the Mate's (`Nova`). |
| A session token reads sensitive service variables as the literal `REDACTED`; an integration token reads them in clear              | The owner's `POST /auth/login` session read `web`'s `GITEA_ADMIN_TOKEN` and the Mate's `GITEA_TOKEN` as `REDACTED` (8 characters) from `GET /service-stack/{id}/user-data`; a `NO_ACCESS` integration token with `BASIC_USER` on the same projects read both in clear (40 characters). Every Gitea call with the redacted value answered `401`. So the broker (an integration token) sees the values it needs, and any client-side read through a person's session does not. The 2026-09-16 row saying the owner reads sensitive values in clear was measured with the zcli token, which is an integration-style token.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| With the broker resolving its admin pair from the platform (gitea-mate v2.1), a fresh account delivers with no restart of anything | Org emptied again, the same driver against gitea-mate `47752ff`: Mate `zcp` ACTIVE 102 s after registration, Gitea `web` and `broker` ACTIVE later, and at 328 s after registration (226 s after the Mate was up) the three variables were on the Mate's service — the broker's first pass once Gitea answered, no restart, no person. The org is left populated for the owner (Gitea, `Imperial Titan - dev`, Mate `Nova`).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |

## Broker-shaped and Mate-shaped tokens against a seconds-old project; writes before the first boot; the create verdict — 2026-09-17

Test org `Mate`, `probe_brokerpush.py` (tag `probe:broker-push-2026-09-17`, four runs, everything deleted, counts equal). A project made the way the client makes a Mate — `POST /client/{id}/project` then `PUT /project/{id}/first-class-recipe/development-container` with the client's `zcp@1` YAML — then, one second later, a `READ_ONLY` token minted with no grants and widened in place to `BASIC_USER` on that project (the broker's shape), and a `NO_ACCESS` + `BASIC_USER` token (a Mate's shape). The research behind D20.
